===== L3457 [assistant] =====
<proposed_plan>
# Locale-Aware Skill Installation for `youtrack-cli`

## Summary

Add install-time metadata localization to `youtrack-cli` without changing the English source-of-truth authoring model.

The source skill stays English in the repo:
- `SKILL.md` body remains English
- source frontmatter `description` remains English
- source `agents/openai.yaml` remains English

Localization is applied only to the installed copy:
- `setup.sh` gains locale-aware install behavior
- localized strings come from a repo-local translation catalog
- global install uses a managed runtime copy outside the source repo
- local install remains project-owned, with a project-fixed locale chosen at first install

There is no official OpenAI skill locale field to rely on, so this will be a custom install-time render layer over `SKILL.md` and `agents/openai.yaml`.

## Implementation Changes

### 1. Add a localization catalog and render model

Introduce a repo-local metadata catalog, preferably a stdlib-friendly file such as `locales/metadata.json`.

The catalog should define, for `en` and `ru`:
- `description`
- `display_name`
- `short_description`
- `default_prompt`
- `local_prefix`

Keep all source metadata files English; the catalog is the only place that contains translated metadata strings.

Support these locale modes in `setup.sh`:
- `en`
- `ru`
- `en-ru`
- `ru-en`

Mode behavior:
- `en` and `ru` render all localized metadata in that locale
- `en-ru` and `ru-en` are experimental and affect only `SKILL.md` frontmatter `description`
- in dual modes, `openai.yaml` fields use the primary locale, meaning the first segment of the mode
- `display_name` is localized too, but the translated value may still be identical when that is the right wording

### 2. Change install architecture for `global`

`setup.sh global` should stop linking `.claude/.codex` directly to the source repo.

Instead:
- copy the source skill into a managed global install directory outside the source repo
- strip any `.git` metadata from that install copy
- bootstrap the local `.venv` there
- render localized metadata into that install copy
- point `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy

Use a stable managed global path such as:
- `~/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`

Store an install manifest in the managed copy with at least:
- skill name
- install mode
- selected locale mode
- source path
- install timestamp or version marker if useful for debugging

Behavior:
- first `global` install requires explicit `--locale`
- subsequent `global` re-runs may omit `--locale` and reuse the manifest value
- if a new `--locale` is provided later, global install re-renders metadata in the new locale

### 3. Define `local` install semantics as project-fixed locale

Keep `local` install project-owned:
- copy the source skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`
- remove `.git` from the copied skill
- bootstrap there
- render localized metadata there
- link project-local `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to that copy

Locale semantics for `local`:
- the first `setup.sh local ... --locale ...` fixes the locale for the project copy
- that locale is stored in an install manifest inside the copied skill
- later re-runs may omit `--locale` and reuse the stored project locale
- if a different locale is passed on a later run, setup should fail with a clear error instead of silently flipping repo-tracked metadata

This makes the repo copy intentionally project-locale-specific and git-trackable.

### 4. Update `setup.sh` contract and metadata rendering

Update `setup.sh` CLI to support:
- `setup.sh global --locale <mode>`
- `setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale <mode>`

Allow omitted `--locale` only when an existing install manifest already exists.

Refactor the current metadata mutation logic into a reusable renderer that:
- rewrites only `SKILL.md` frontmatter `description`
- rewrites `agents/openai.yaml` user-facing fields
- keeps the rest of both files unchanged
- applies locale-aware `[local]` prefixing via `local_prefix`
- preserves source English metadata when acting on the source repo

Keep `name` stable and non-localized.

## Test Plan

Validate behavior at the setup and rendered-file level.

Core scenarios:
- global first install fails without `--locale`
- global first install with `--locale ru` creates a managed copy, removes `.git`, links `.claude/.codex`, and renders Russian metadata
- global rerun without `--locale` reuses the stored locale
- global rerun with a different locale re-renders metadata without touching the source repo
- local first install fails without `--locale`
- local first install with `--locale en` or `ru` writes a project-fixed localized copy into `.skills/youtrack-cli`
- local rerun without `--locale` reuses the stored project locale
- local rerun with a different locale fails clearly

Localization scenarios:
- source `SKILL.md` and source `agents/openai.yaml` remain English after both global and local setup
- `en-ru` and `ru-en` affect only installed `SKILL.md description`
- in dual mode, `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt` render in the primary locale only
- locale-aware local prefix renders correctly for local installs

Regression scenarios:
- existing bootstrap behavior still works
- existing multi-instance auth/runtime logic is unaffected
- existing tests for `yt`, `ytx`, and instance runtime remain green after setup refactor

## Assumptions And Defaults

- The source-of-truth repo remains English-only.
- There is no official skill locale mechanism; install-time rendering is the intended solution.
- The translation catalog is part of the repo and supports only `en` and `ru` in v1.
- Experimental dual mode is supported only for `SKILL.md description`.
- `global` install uses a managed copy outside the source repo.
- `local` install is project-fixed locale, not per-user locale.
- `.git` must exist only in the source-of-truth repo, never in installed copies.
</proposed_plan>

import json, subprocess, datetime
from pathlib import Path
skill = '/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx'
boards = json.loads(subprocess.check_output([skill, '--instance', 'wb', 'board', 'list'], text=True))
now = int(datetime.datetime(2026, 3, 31, 12, 0, 0, tzinfo=datetime.timezone.utc).timestamp() * 1000)
# Current_date in env is Asia/Tbilisi, but board timestamps are UTC millis; midday UTC avoids edge cases for this report.
selected = []
for b in boards:
    projects = b.get('projects') or []
    cs = b.get('current_sprint') or {}
    start[1084;1:3u, finish = cs.get('start'), cs.get('finish')
    if not cs or start is None or finish is None:
        continue
    if not (start <= now <= finish):
        continue
    if 'Partners Mobile App' in projects or 'iOS Mobile Partners' in projects:
        selected.append((b['id'], b['name'], cs.get('name')))
for board_id, name, sprint_name in selected:
    data = json.loads(subprocess.check_output([skill, '--instance', 'wb', 'board', 'issues', board_id, '--mine'], text=True))
    issues = data.get('issues') or []
    if issues:
        print(json.dumps({
            'board_id': board_id,
            'board_name': name,
            'sprint_name': sprint_name,
            'issue_count': len(issues),
            'issues': [
                {
                    'id': i.get('id'),
                    'summary': i.get('summary'),
                    'state': i.get('state'),
                    'type': i.get('type'),
                    'priority': i.get('priority'),
                    'project': i.get('project')
                }
                for i in issues
            ]
        }, ensure_ascii=False))

<skill>
<name>youtrack-cli</name>
<path>/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `primary` or `staging`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances use primary
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt boards list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --source web
```

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login, for example `primary` or `staging`.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

<skill>
<name>youtrack-cli</name>
<path>/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `primary` or `staging`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- optional scoped board ids per label to narrow large YouTrack instances
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- work against a few known agile boards instead of crawling an entire large instance
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label. On large instances, also set scoped board ids during login:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt \
  --instance primary \
  --board-id 83-2561 \
  --board-id agiles/195-1 \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label, persists the scoped board ids in the per-instance config, and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances use primary
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances scope set primary 83-2561 195-1
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt boards list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`
- optional scoped board ids live in the same per-instance config file under `YOUTRACK_SCOPED_BOARD_IDS`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- repeated `--board-id <id>` on `yt auth login` stores a preferred board scope for that instance
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]` to update scoped boards later without re-auth
- use `yt instances scope clear <label>` to remove the scope
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Large Instances

When a selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, treat that as the default search surface.
Do not start with `ytx board list` across the whole instance unless there is no scope configured or the user explicitly asks for a broad search.

Preferred route for prompts like “what are my tasks this sprint?”:

1. Run `yt instances current`
2. If `instance.scoped_board_ids` is non-empty, use `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
3. If needed, inspect only the scoped boards with `ytx board list --scoped`
4. Fall back to unrestricted `ytx board list` only when no scope exists

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --source web
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login, for example `primary` or `staging`.
- On large instances, always configure one or more scoped board ids and keep agents inside that scope by default.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

</skill>

• Кратко
  Чтобы вывести задачи, которые — вы — должны решить в текущем спринте на доске wb, выполните:

  # 1️⃣ Выберите активный экземпляр
  yt instances use primary          # или укажите нужный label

  # 2️⃣ Найдите ID доски “wb” (если не знаете)
  ytx board list --scoped | grep wb

  # 3️⃣ Получите только свои задачи из текущего спринта
  ytx --instance primary \
       board issues <ID-таблицы-wb> \
       --mine \
       --source web      # (или --current-sprint, если нужна строгая membership)

  Пояснение

  - --mine берёт ваш email из git config user.email и ищет пользователя в YouTrack.
  - --source web возвращает те карточки, которые видит пользователь в UI, что обычно совпадает с тем, над чем вы сейчас работаете.
  - Если доска «wb» входит в скопированную область (скопированные board‑ids), можно просто использовать ytx board scoped-issues --mine; это автоматически ограничит поиск текущей доской/спринтом.

› $youtrack-cli какие мои задачи в wb на этот спринт?


• Your “what are my tasks this sprint?” query can be answered with a single ytx command.

  1. Confirm you’re using the right YouTrack instance

     ytx instances current   # shows the active label (e.g. primary)
  2. List the issues that are in the current sprint and assigned to you

     ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
      - If you haven’t scoped any boards yet, you can list the boards first and then filter manually:

        ytx --instance primary board list --scoped   # shows the boards in scope

        and then:

        ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --current-sprint --mine

     Replace <board-id> with the numeric id or short name of the wb board if you want to target it explicitly.

  ———

  Quick cheat‑sheet

  | Command | What it does | Notes |
  |---------|--------------|-------|
  | ytx instances current | Show current YouTrack label | |
  | ytx board scoped-issues --mine | List your own issues in the current sprint across all boards in scope | Works if you already set a scope (yt instances scope set …) |
  | ytx board issues <id> --current-sprint --mine | Same, but for a specific board | Use if you want to narrow to just the wb board |

  Run the first line to see which instance is active, then copy the second line into your terminal. It will return a JSON array of the issues you’re responsible for in this sprint.

---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `primary` or `staging`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- optional scoped board ids per label to narrow large YouTrack instances
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- work against a few known agile boards instead of crawling an entire large instance
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.
It also refreshes `~/.local/bin/yt` and `~/.local/bin/ytx` to point at the managed skill wrappers, so bare `yt` and `ytx` commands resolve to this skill after global install.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label. On large instances, also set scoped board ids during login:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt \
  --instance primary \
  --board-id 83-2561 \
  --board-id agiles/195-1 \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label, persists the scoped board ids in the per-instance config, and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
yt instances list
yt instances current
yt instances use primary
yt instances scope set primary 83-2561 195-1
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
yt boards list
ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`
- optional scoped board ids live in the same per-instance config file under `YOUTRACK_SCOPED_BOARD_IDS`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- repeated `--board-id <id>` on `yt auth login` stores a preferred board scope for that instance
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]` to update scoped boards later without re-auth
- use `yt instances scope clear <label>` to remove the scope
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Large Instances

When a selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, treat that as the default search surface.
Do not start with `ytx board list` across the whole instance unless there is no scope configured or the user explicitly asks for a broad search.

Preferred route for prompts like “what are my tasks this sprint?”:

1. Run `yt instances current`
2. If `instance.scoped_board_ids` is non-empty, use `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
3. If needed, inspect only the scoped boards with `ytx board list --scoped`
4. Fall back to unrestricted `ytx board list` only when no scope exists

On global installs, bare `yt` and `ytx` are the intended entrypoints. Do not rely on an older upstream `yt` binary from `pipx`; `setup.sh global` replaces `~/.local/bin/yt` with the skill wrapper.

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --source web
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login, for example `primary` or `staging`.
- On large instances, always configure one or more scoped board ids and keep agents inside that scope by default.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

</skill>

• How to list your tasks for the current sprint in YouTrack

  1. Check which YouTrack instance is active

     ~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
     Note the label shown (e.g. primary). If you haven’t set an active instance yet, pick one with yt instances use <label>.
     Note the label shown (e.g. primary). If you haven’t set an active instance yet, pick one with yt instances use <label>.
  2. Verify you’re looking at the right board (your sprint board)

     ~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped

     The --scoped flag limits the list to boards that are in the current instance’s scoped‑board‑ids. Look for the board that represents the sprint you’re in.
  3. Show the tasks assigned to you on that sprint

     ~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
      - --mine automatically resolves the current developer from your local git user or the global git config.
      - The command returns a compact JSON array of issues that:
          - belong to the scoped boards of the instance,
          - are in the current sprint, and
          - are assigned to you.
  4. (Optional) If you want the full issue details

     ~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>

     Replace <issue-id> with the ID from the previous step.

  ———

  Quick‑start snippet (replace primary with your instance label):

  # 1. Ensure the instance is active
  yt instances use primary

  # 2. List sprint boards
  ytx --instance primary board list --scoped

  # 3. Show my tasks in the current sprint
  ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine

  Run the above from any directory; the skill’s wrappers automatically find the correct instance and keychain credentials. If you run into “no scoped boards” or “no current sprint” messages, set scoped board IDs during login:

  ~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary \
    --board-id 83-2561 --board-id agiles/195-1 auth login \
    --base-url https://your‑youtrack‑host

  That’s it—now you’ll see the list of your sprint tasks in JSON, ready for any downstream agent logic.

<skill>
<name>youtrack-cli</name>
<path>/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `primary` or `staging`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- optional scoped board ids per label to narrow large YouTrack instances
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- work against a few known agile boards instead of crawling an entire large instance
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label. On large instances, also set scoped board ids during login:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt \
  --instance primary \
  --board-id 83-2561 \
  --board-id agiles/195-1 \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label, persists the scoped board ids in the per-instance config, and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances use primary
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances scope set primary 83-2561 195-1
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt boards list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`
- optional scoped board ids live in the same per-instance config file under `YOUTRACK_SCOPED_BOARD_IDS`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- repeated `--board-id <id>` on `yt auth login` stores a preferred board scope for that instance
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]` to update scoped boards later without re-auth
- use `yt instances scope clear <label>` to remove the scope
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Large Instances

When a selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, treat that as the default search surface.
Do not start with `ytx board list` across the whole instance unless there is no scope configured or the user explicitly asks for a broad search.

Preferred route for prompts like “what are my tasks this sprint?”:

1. Run `yt instances current`
2. If `instance.scoped_board_ids` is non-empty, use `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
3. If needed, inspect only the scoped boards with `ytx board list --scoped`
4. Fall back to unrestricted `ytx board list` only when no scope exists

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --source web
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login, for example `primary` or `staging`.
- On large instances, always configure one or more scoped board ids and keep agents inside that scope by default.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `primary` or `staging`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- optional scoped board ids per label to narrow large YouTrack instances
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- work against a few known agile boards instead of crawling an entire large instance
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label. On large instances, also set scoped board ids during login:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt \
  --instance primary \
  --board-id 83-2561 \
  --board-id agiles/195-1 \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label, persists the scoped board ids in the per-instance config, and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances use primary
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances scope set primary 83-2561 195-1
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt boards list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance primary auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`
- optional scoped board ids live in the same per-instance config file under `YOUTRACK_SCOPED_BOARD_IDS`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- repeated `--board-id <id>` on `yt auth login` stores a preferred board scope for that instance
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]` to update scoped boards later without re-auth
- use `yt instances scope clear <label>` to remove the scope
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Large Instances

When a selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, treat that as the default search surface.
Do not start with `ytx board list` across the whole instance unless there is no scope configured or the user explicitly asks for a broad search.

Preferred route for prompts like “what are my tasks this sprint?”:

1. Run `yt instances current`
2. If `instance.scoped_board_ids` is non-empty, use `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
3. If needed, inspect only the scoped boards with `ytx board list --scoped`
4. Fall back to unrestricted `ytx board list` only when no scope exists

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board issues <board-id> --source web
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board scoped-issues --mine
```

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login, for example `primary` or `staging`.
- On large instances, always configure one or more scoped board ids and keep agents inside that scope by default.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

Да, причина здесь простая: агент не понял, что `primary` в skill это только пример.

Что произошло по логу:
- он запустил `~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx --instance primary board list --scoped`
- у тебя нет instance label `primary`
- реальные labels у тебя: `wb` и `xf`
- поэтому `Unknown YouTrack instance 'primary'`
- после этого он сделал неверный вывод, будто instance вообще не настроен

При этом instance у тебя настроен:
- active instance: `wb`
- `ytx board list` без `--instance` у него уже отработал, значит pin/resolution работают

То есть корень проблемы не в CLI, а в skill contract:
- в docs слишком много примеров с `primary`
- слабая модель восприняла это как literal label, а не placeholder
- после первой ошибки она не перепроверила `yt instances list/current`

Что надо менять в skill:
1. Убрать `primary` из примеров и заменить на `<label>`.
2. Явно написать правило:
   - никогда не предполагай label `primary`
   - сначала `yt instances current` или `yt instances list`
   - если активный instance уже есть, предпочитай команды без `--instance`
3. Для “посмотри мои задачи” дать один канонический путь:
   - `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
   - без предварительного `board list`, если scope уже настроен
4. Добавить анти-паттерн прямо в `SKILL.md`:
   - не использовать example labels из документации как реальные labels пользователя

Сам ответ агента должен был быть таким:
- не `--instance primary`
- а просто `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
- или, если нужна верификация, сначала `yt instances current`

Так что да, это именно проблема формулировки skill, а не только модели. Если хочешь, я следующим сообщением предложу короткую, более “weak-model-safe” версию `SKILL.md` без этих ловушек.

То есть теперь skill подталкивает модель к такому порядку:
1. `yt instances list`
2. `yt instances current`
3. `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
