---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Overview

This skill provides a portable YouTrack CLI runtime inside the skill directory.
Use it when an agent or developer needs deterministic command-line access to YouTrack boards, sprints, issues, comments, and issue mutations without introducing an MCP server.

The skill bundles two entrypoints:

- `scripts/yt` for the upstream `youtrack-cli` project by Ryan Cheley
- `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing JSON operations layered on top of the same credentials

Both wrappers resolve paths relative to the skill location, so they work from any current directory and do not depend on a user-specific `pipx` path.
They share the same multi-instance runtime:

- named `instance` labels such as `<label>`
- one Keychain service per label
- one config file per label in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/`
- optional scoped board ids per label to narrow large YouTrack instances
- one pinned active instance per installed skill copy

## When To Use

Use this skill when you need one or more of the following:

- inspect agile boards, current sprints, and board-visible issues
- search, view, comment on, or update YouTrack issues
- add or remove issues from explicit board/sprint membership
- resolve the current developer from local git config and map that identity to a YouTrack user
- keep authentication in macOS Keychain instead of shell history, env files, or repo config
- work against a few known agile boards instead of crawling an entire large instance
- switch cleanly between multiple YouTrack instances without overwriting credentials

Do not use this skill when the user explicitly wants an MCP integration instead of a local CLI workflow.

## Resolve Context First

Always start by resolving the current environment before issuing any YouTrack command that depends on an instance label or board scope:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
```

Use these two commands first in this order:

1. `yt instances list` tells you which labels actually exist on this machine
2. `yt instances current` tells you which label is active for this installed copy

In all command examples below:

- `<label>` means a real label from `yt instances list`
- `<board-id>` means a real agile board id
- `<issue-id>` means a real YouTrack issue id

## Quick Start

1. Install the skill into your agent environments:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh global --locale en
```

For a single repository instead of your home-level agent config:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale ru
```

`global` creates a managed install copy outside the source repo and links `~/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `~/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` to that managed copy.

`local` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, removes any nested git metadata from that copy, and then points `<repo>/.claude/skills/youtrack-cli` and `<repo>/.codex/skills/youtrack-cli` at the copied version. That makes the skill part of the project repo instead of a symlink to your home directory.

The source skill stays English. Installed copies render `description` and other user-facing metadata from `locales/metadata.json` for the selected locale mode. Supported locale modes are `en`, `ru`, `en-ru`, and `ru-en`.

2. Authenticate with YouTrack. Login now requires an explicit label. On large instances, also set scoped board ids during login:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt \
  --instance <label> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

This stores the token in macOS Keychain under the selected label, persists the scoped board ids in the per-instance config, and auto-pins that label for the current install.

3. Inspect and pin instances:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances use <label>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]
```

4. Use `yt` for raw CLI coverage and `ytx` for stable JSON reads and common mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt boards list
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

For self-signed or custom CA deployments, pass the same SSL options supported by `youtrack-cli`, for example:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance <label> auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --cert-file /path/to/cert.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance <label> auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --ca-bundle /path/to/ca-bundle.pem
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/yt --instance <label> auth login --base-url https://your-youtrack-host --no-verify-ssl
```

## Runtime Layout

- `setup.sh global` creates a managed install copy under `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/agents/skills/youtrack-cli`, bootstraps that copy, renders localized metadata, and links `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.codex/skills` to it
- `setup.sh local <repo>` copies the skill into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`, strips git metadata from that copy, bootstraps the copied runtime, renders localized metadata, and then links the project's `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` to the copied skill
- `scripts/bootstrap.sh` creates `.venv/` inside the installed skill and installs the pinned dependencies from `scripts/requirements.txt`
- `scripts/yt` is a Python-aware wrapper around the upstream MIT-licensed `youtrack-cli` package and injects the selected instance config/keychain context
- `scripts/ytx` runs the bundled Python helper from that environment
- `scripts/ytx.py` is the agent-facing wrapper implementation
- `locales/metadata.json` is the source of truth for installed localized metadata
- per-instance config files live in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/instances/<label>.env`
- per-install pin state lives in `~/.config/youtrack-cli/installs/<install-id>.json`
- optional scoped board ids live in the same per-instance config file under `YOUTRACK_SCOPED_BOARD_IDS`

The wrappers auto-bootstrap if the environment is missing, but `setup.sh` is the preferred first step because it both prepares the runtime and links the skill into the selected agent environments.
Local installs still keep their pinned instance state outside the repo; only the skill code is copied into `<repo>/.skills/youtrack-cli`. Local install locale is project-fixed after the first install. Global install locale may be changed on a later rerun.

## Multi-instance Rules

- `yt auth login` requires `--instance <label>`
- repeated `--board-id <id>` on `yt auth login` stores a preferred board scope for that instance
- default resolution for commands is: `--instance` > `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE` > pinned active instance for this install > the only registered instance
- start every session with `yt instances list` and then `yt instances current`
- use `yt instances use <label>` to pin a default for the current install
- use `yt instances scope set <label> <board-id> [<board-id> ...]` to update scoped boards later without re-auth
- use `yt instances scope clear <label>` to remove the scope
- use `YOUTRACK_INSTANCE=<label>` for one shell or one agent run without changing the pin
- use `--no-auto-pin` with `yt --instance <label> auth login` if login should not change the current pin
- legacy single-account `~/.config/youtrack-cli/.env` is intentionally ignored; re-login under an explicit label if you were using the old setup

## Large Instances

When a selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, treat that as the default search surface.
Do not start with `ytx board list` across the whole instance unless there is no scope configured or the user explicitly asks for a broad search.

Preferred route for prompts like “what are my tasks this sprint?”:

1. Run `yt instances list`
2. Run `yt instances current`
3. If `instance.scoped_board_ids` is non-empty, use `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
4. If needed, inspect only the scoped boards with `ytx board list --scoped`
5. Fall back to unrestricted `ytx board list` only when no scope exists

## Core Workflows

### Read Boards And Sprint Issues

Use `ytx` for JSON-friendly reads:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board list --scoped
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board show <board-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board sprints <board-id> --current
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --source web
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

Use the no-`--instance` form when `yt instances current` already points at the right label.
Use `--instance <label>` only after resolving `<label>` from `yt instances list/current`.

Default board issue source is `web`, because some explicit/manual agile boards show issue cards in the UI that are not returned by the strict sprint membership endpoint. Read [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) when a board appears inconsistent.

### Filter To The Current Developer

Use `--mine` for board queries:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to the global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value. If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with the candidate list instead of guessing.

### Inspect And Mutate Issues

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Текст комментария'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
```

For unsupported or project-specific commands, use the raw command interface:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important'
```

Use `--dry-run` before risky command mutations:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>' --dry-run
```

### Manage Board Membership Explicitly

For boards with explicit sprint membership, use:

```bash
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
~/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
```

This compiles down to a YouTrack command such as:

```text
add Board <board-name> <sprint-name>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain via `yt auth login`; do not save them in shell history or checked-in files.
- Always choose an explicit label on first login.
- On large instances, always configure one or more scoped board ids and keep agents inside that scope by default.
- Prefer `ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits compact JSON.
- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- When a board UI and the strict sprint endpoint disagree, trust `ytx board issues --source web` for "what the user currently sees on the board", and use the strict endpoint only when you specifically need sprint membership.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

===== L4796 [assistant] =====
Потому что `SKILL.md` всё ещё написан больше как `how-to для человека`, чем как `операционный контракт для агента`.

Слабая модель читает его так:
- “вот команды, которые надо предложить пользователю”
- а не так:
- “вот команды, которые я должен выполнить сам”

Конкретно у тебя сейчас это провоцируют такие вещи:
- много блоков `Quick Start` и shell-примеров
- формулировки вида `Use ...`, `Run ...`, `Find the board ID`
- нет явного правила `если пользователь спрашивает про свои задачи, выполни команды сам и верни результат`
- нет явного правила `не проси пользователя запускать команды, если auth уже настроен`
- нет жёсткого happy path для запроса `мои задачи на wb доске`

То есть модель не “ленится”, она просто выбирает более безопасный режим: инструкцию вместо действия.

Для weak-model-safe версии нужно добавить именно такие правила:

1. `Default Behavior`
- если пользователь просит посмотреть задачи, спринт, комментарии, статус, доску, агент должен сам запускать `yt`/`ytx` и возвращать результат
- не выводить пользователю команды, если только он явно не попросил инструкции

2. `Task Shortcut`
- для запросов вроде `мои задачи`, `мои задачи на wb`, `что у меня на спринте`
  порядок должен быть жёсткий:
  - `yt instances list`
  - `yt instances current`
  - если есть `scoped_board_ids`, сразу `ytx board scoped-issues --mine`
  - только если это не дало ответа, идти в `board list --scoped`

3. `When To Ask`
- спрашивать пользователя только если:
  - нет ни одного instance
  - не настроен active instance и их несколько
  - нет scoped boards и нельзя понять нужную доску
  - auth не готов

4. `Output Policy`
- по умолчанию отвечать результатом
- команды показывать только как secondary fallback

То есть проблема не в `primary` как таковом. Это был один из триггеров. Главная проблема в том, что skill пока не говорит достаточно жёстко: `execute, don't instruct`.

Если хочешь, я следующим сообщением прямо перепишу `SKILL.md` в этот формат: короткий, low-freedom, с явным `default execution mode` и без tutorial-style секций.

# YouTrack CLI

## Default Mode

- Execute the bundled commands yourself and return the result.
- Do not answer with a shell tutorial when the skill is already installed and authentication exists.
- Show commands only when the user explicitly asks for instructions or when setup/auth is missing.
- Use `scripts/yt` and `scripts/ytx` from this skill directory, not a bare `yt` from `PATH`.

## Resolve Context First

Always begin with these two commands in this order:

```bash
scripts/yt instances list
scripts/yt instances current
```

Use the result to choose the instance context:

- If the user names a label and it exists, use `--instance <label>` for this turn.
- If `instances current` already points at the correct label, prefer commands without `--instance`.
- If several labels exist and there is no active instance, ask which label to use.
- If no labels exist, stop and ask the user to log in first.

In all commands below:

- `<label>` is a real label from `scripts/yt instances list`
- `<board-id>` is a real agile board id
- `<issue-id>` is a real issue id

## Fast Path: My Tasks

For prompts like:

- "какие мои задачи"
- "мои задачи на доске"
- "what are my tasks this sprint"

use this order:

1. Run `scripts/yt instances list`
2. Run `scripts/yt instances current`
3. If the selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, run:

```bash
scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

4. If the user asked about a specific board and the scoped result is missing or ambiguous:

```bash
scripts/ytx board list --scoped
scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --mine --source web
```

5. Only if there is no scope, broaden to:

```bash
scripts/ytx board list
```

Return the actual tasks. Do not ask the user to run these commands unless the skill is not installed or auth is missing.

## Scope Rules For Large Instances

- If `scoped_board_ids` is configured for the selected instance, treat that scope as the default search surface.
- Do not start with unrestricted `scripts/ytx board list` on large instances.
- Prefer `scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine` for developer-focused reads.
- Prefer `scripts/ytx board list --scoped` before any full-instance board enumeration.

## Board Reads

Use `scripts/ytx` for agent-facing reads:

```bash
scripts/ytx board list --scoped
scripts/ytx board show <board-id>
scripts/ytx board sprints <board-id> --current
scripts/ytx board issues <board-id> --source web
scripts/ytx board scoped-issues --mine
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--source web` when the user asks what is visible on the board.
- Use the strict sprint endpoint only when the user explicitly asks about sprint membership.
- Use `--mine` when the request is about the current developer.

## Issue Reads

```bash
scripts/ytx issue show <issue-id>
scripts/ytx issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
scripts/ytx issue comment-list <issue-id>
```

## Mutations

Use the wrappers directly for common writes:

```bash
scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
scripts/ytx issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
scripts/ytx issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Comment text'
scripts/ytx issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
scripts/ytx issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
scripts/ytx issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important' --dry-run
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- For board membership on explicit boards, use `board-add` and `board-remove` instead of guessing field updates.

## Current Developer Resolution

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value.

If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with candidate users. In that case, ask the user which account to use.

## Ask Only If Blocked

Ask the user only when one of these is true:

- no YouTrack instances are configured
- the requested label does not exist
- several labels exist and there is no active instance
- no scoped board matches the request and the target board cannot be identified
- a write target is missing or ambiguous

Otherwise, run the commands and return the result.

## Setup Fallback

Use this only when installation or authentication is missing.

Install globally:

```bash
setup.sh global --locale <locale>
```

Install into one repository:

```bash
setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale <locale>
```

Log in with an explicit label and optional scoped boards:

```bash
scripts/yt \
  --instance <label> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

Pin the active instance:

```bash
scripts/yt instances use <label>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain through `scripts/yt auth login`.
- Do not save tokens in shell history, env files, or checked-in files.
- Use an explicit label for each instance.
- On large instances, keep agents inside scoped boards by default.
- Prefer `scripts/ytx` for reads consumed by agents because it emits stable JSON.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Default Mode

- Execute the bundled commands yourself and return the result.
- Do not answer with a shell tutorial when the skill is already installed and authentication exists.
- Show commands only when the user explicitly asks for instructions or when setup/auth is missing.
- Resolve command paths from this skill file path.
- If the skill file path is `/abs/path/to/SKILL.md`, then:
  - `<yt-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/yt`
  - `<ytx-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/ytx`
- Use those absolute command paths for every command in this skill.
- Do not run `scripts/yt` or `scripts/ytx` as paths relative to the current working directory.
- Do not use a bare `yt` from `PATH`.

## Resolve Context First

Always begin with these two commands in this order:

```bash
<yt-command> instances list
<yt-command> instances current
```

Use the result to choose the instance context:

- If the user names a label and it exists, use `--instance <label>` for this turn.
- If `instances current` already points at the correct label, prefer commands without `--instance`.
- If several labels exist and there is no active instance, ask which label to use.
- If no labels exist, stop and ask the user to log in first.

In all commands below:

- `<label>` is a real label from `<yt-command> instances list`
- `<board-id>` is a real agile board id
- `<issue-id>` is a real issue id

## Fast Path: My Tasks

For prompts like:

- "какие мои задачи"
- "мои задачи на доске"
- "what are my tasks this sprint"

use this order:

1. Run `<yt-command> instances list`
2. Run `<yt-command> instances current`
3. If the user asks about current sprint or "my tasks" without naming a board, and the selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, run:

```bash
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

4. If the user asked about a specific board, use board-specific reads for the final answer. When the selected instance has scoped boards, identify the board inside that scope first:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web
```

5. Only if there is no scope, broaden to:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list
```

Return the actual tasks. Do not ask the user to run these commands unless the skill is not installed or auth is missing.

If the selected instance has exactly one scoped board and the user refers to "the board" loosely, treat that single scoped board as the target board.

## Scope Rules For Large Instances

- If `scoped_board_ids` is configured for the selected instance, treat that scope as the default search surface.
- Do not start with unrestricted `<ytx-command> board list` on large instances.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for developer-focused reads.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board list --scoped` before any full-instance board enumeration.

## Board Reads

Use `<ytx-command>` for agent-facing reads:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board show <board-id>
<ytx-command> board sprints <board-id> --current
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --source web
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--source web` when the user asks what is visible on the board.
- Use the strict sprint endpoint only when the user explicitly asks about sprint membership.
- Use `--mine` when the request is about the current developer.
- For a board-specific question, prefer `<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web` over `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for the final answer.

## Issue Reads

```bash
<ytx-command> issue show <issue-id>
<ytx-command> issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
<ytx-command> issue comment-list <issue-id>
```

## Mutations

Use the wrappers directly for common writes:

```bash
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
<ytx-command> issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Comment text'
<ytx-command> issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
<ytx-command> issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
<ytx-command> issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important' --dry-run
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- For board membership on explicit boards, use `board-add` and `board-remove` instead of guessing field updates.

## Current Developer Resolution

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value.

If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with candidate users. In that case, ask the user which account to use.

## Ask Only If Blocked

Ask the user only when one of these is true:

- no YouTrack instances are configured
- the requested label does not exist
- several labels exist and there is no active instance
- no scoped board matches the request and the target board cannot be identified
- a write target is missing or ambiguous

Otherwise, run the commands and return the result.

## Output Contract

When answering a read query, return the result itself, not a command recipe.

For task lists on a board or sprint:

- include board name
- include sprint name when available
- include total matching issue count
- list every matching issue id, summary, state, and type
- do not omit `Done` issues unless the user explicitly asks for only active or unresolved work

For a single issue:

- include id, summary, state, type, priority, assignee, and the main description

## Setup Fallback

Use this only when installation or authentication is missing.

Install globally:

```bash
setup.sh global --locale <locale>
```

Install into one repository:

```bash
setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale <locale>
```

Log in with an explicit label and optional scoped boards:

```bash
<yt-command> \
  --instance <label> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

Pin the active instance:

```bash
<yt-command> instances use <label>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain through `<yt-command> auth login`.
- Do not save tokens in shell history, env files, or checked-in files.
- Use an explicit label for each instance.
- On large instances, keep agents inside scoped boards by default.
- Prefer `<ytx-command>` for reads consumed by agents because it emits stable JSON.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

===== L5109 [assistant] =====
Да. Это как раз правильное направление: меньше свободы у модели, больше смысла в самой команде.

Сейчас у нас большая часть логики живёт в `SKILL.md`:
- сначала резолв instance
- потом понять scope
- потом выбрать `scoped-issues` или `board issues`
- потом не потерять `Done`
- потом правильно оформить ответ

Это всё ещё слишком много решений для слабой модели. Лучше переносить их в tool surface.

**1. Отдельная команда `ytx board my-tasks`**

Это самый практичный следующий шаг.

Что она должна делать внутри себя:
- выбрать instance через текущий runtime
- если есть `scoped_board_ids`:
  - если scope ровно один, взять его
  - если scope несколько, либо вернуть grouped result по boards, либо принять `--board`
- резолвить `--mine`
- по умолчанию использовать `source=web`
- вернуть уже готовый payload для ответа

Пример интерфейса:
```bash
ytx board my-tasks
ytx board my-tasks --board 83-2561
ytx board my-tasks --board 'PMA iOS Core'
ytx board my-tasks --active-only
ytx board my-tasks --source strict
```

Что это даст:
- модель не будет сама выбирать между `scoped-issues` и `board issues`
- не будет лишних промежуточных вызовов
- не будет повторного чтения одних и тех же данных
- легче сделать один стабильный output contract

Я бы возвращал сразу такой JSON:
```json
{
  "board_id": "83-2561",
  "board_name": "PMA iOS Core",
  "sprint_id": "84-96818",
  "sprint_name": "Спринт 47",
  "assignee": "oparin.ivan3",
  "issue_count": 5,
  "issues": [
    {"id":"PMA-21079","summary":"...","state":"In Progress","type":"Epic"}
  ]
}
```

То есть не `boards: [...]`, а уже answer-shaped payload.

**2. Компактный `q`-слой**

Это следующий уровень. Не одна специальная команда, а маленький agent DSL поверх чтения.

Идея:
- вместо десятка subcommands у агента один entrypoint
- он выражает, что именно хочет получить
- tool сам ограничивает поля и структуру ответа

Пример:
```bash
ytx q 'my_tasks() { board_name sprint_name issues[id summary state type] }'
ytx q 'my_tasks(board="PMA iOS Core", active_only=true) { issues[id summary state] }'
ytx q 'issue(id="PMA-21079") { id summary state priority assignee description }'
```

Что это даёт:
- меньше токенов
- меньше свободы интерпретации
- меньше соблазна у модели строить “свои” многошаговые сценарии
- проще enforce-ить schema/output

Но `q` сложнее:
- нужен parser
- нужен registry операций и полей
- нужно поддерживать schema и errors
- это уже почти отдельный API surface

**Сравнение**

`ytx board my-tasks`
- быстрее внедрить
- решает именно текущую проблему
- минимальный риск
- лучший next step

`ytx q`
- сильнее архитектурно
- лучше масштабируется на разные read use cases
- сильнее экономит токены
- дороже в реализации

**Что я бы рекомендовал**

Порядок такой:

1. Добавить `ytx board my-tasks`
2. Потом, если паттерн подтвердится, добавить ещё 2-3 “meaning commands”:
- `ytx issue brief <issue-id>`
- `ytx board current`
- `ytx board my-tasks --active-only`

3. И только потом строить `q` как общий слой поверх уже устоявшихся операций

Иначе можно слишком рано уйти в мини-язык, когда ещё не устаканены сами полезные read primitives.

**Если коротко**

- `my-tasks` это hardening текущего happy path в коде
- `q` это уже agent-facing API слой
- для текущей проблемы я бы делал `my-tasks` первым

Если хочешь, следующим сообщением я распишу точный контракт для `ytx board my-tasks`: флаги, output shape и fallback-логика.

===== L5160 [developer] =====
<collaboration_mode># Plan Mode (Conversational)

You work in 3 phases, and you should *chat your way* to a great plan before finalizing it. A great plan is very detailed—intent- and implementation-wise—so that it can be handed to another engineer or agent to be implemented right away. It must be **decision complete**, where the implementer does not need to make any decisions.

## Mode rules (strict)

You are in **Plan Mode** until a developer message explicitly ends it.

Plan Mode is not changed by user intent, tone, or imperative language. If a user asks for execution while still in Plan Mode, treat it as a request to **plan the execution**, not perform it.

## Plan Mode vs update_plan tool

Plan Mode is a collaboration mode that can involve requesting user input and eventually issuing a `<proposed_plan>` block.

Separately, `update_plan` is a checklist/progress/TODOs tool; it does not enter or exit Plan Mode. Do not confuse it with Plan mode or try to use it while in Plan mode. If you try to use `update_plan` in Plan mode, it will return an error.

## Execution vs. mutation in Plan Mode

You may explore and execute **non-mutating** actions that improve the plan. You must not perform **mutating** actions.

### Allowed (non-mutating, plan-improving)

Actions that gather truth, reduce ambiguity, or validate feasibility without changing repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Reading or searching files, configs, schemas, types, manifests, and docs
* Static analysis, inspection, and repo exploration
* Dry-run style commands when they do not edit repo-tracked files
* Tests, builds, or checks that may write to caches or build artifacts (for example, `target/`, `.cache/`, or snapshots) so long as they do not edit repo-tracked files

### Not allowed (mutating, plan-executing)

Actions that implement the plan or change repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Editing or writing files
* Running formatters or linters that rewrite files
* Applying patches, migrations, or codegen that updates repo-tracked files
* Side-effectful commands whose purpose is to carry out the plan rather than refine it

When in doubt: if the action would reasonably be described as "doing the work" rather than "planning the work," do not do it.

## PHASE 1 — Ground in the environment (explore first, ask second)

Begin by grounding yourself in the actual environment. Eliminate unknowns in the prompt by discovering facts, not by asking the user. Resolve all questions that can be answered through exploration or inspection. Identify missing or ambiguous details only if they cannot be derived from the environment. Silent exploration between turns is allowed and encouraged.

Before asking the user any question, perform at least one targeted non-mutating exploration pass (for example: search relevant files, inspect likely entrypoints/configs, confirm current implementation shape), unless no local environment/repo is available.

Exception: you may ask clarifying questions about the user's prompt before exploring, ONLY if there are obvious ambiguities or contradictions in the prompt itself. However, if ambiguity might be resolved by exploring, always prefer exploring first.

Do not ask questions that can be answered from the repo or system (for example, "where is this struct?" or "which UI component should we use?" when exploration can make it clear). Only ask once you have exhausted reasonable non-mutating exploration.

## PHASE 2 — Intent chat (what they actually want)

* Keep asking until you can clearly state: goal + success criteria, audience, in/out of scope, constraints, current state, and the key preferences/tradeoffs.
* Bias toward questions over guessing: if any high-impact ambiguity remains, do NOT plan yet—ask.

## PHASE 3 — Implementation chat (what/how we’ll build)

* Once intent is stable, keep asking until the spec is decision complete: approach, interfaces (APIs/schemas/I/O), data flow, edge cases/failure modes, testing + acceptance criteria, rollout/monitoring, and any migrations/compat constraints.

## Asking questions

Critical rules:

* Strongly prefer using the `request_user_input` tool to ask any questions.
* Offer only meaningful multiple‑choice options; don’t include filler choices that are obviously wrong or irrelevant.
* In rare cases where an unavoidable, important question can’t be expressed with reasonable multiple‑choice options (due to extreme ambiguity), you may ask it directly without the tool.

You SHOULD ask many questions, but each question must:

* materially change the spec/plan, OR
* confirm/lock an assumption, OR
* choose between meaningful tradeoffs.
* not be answerable by non-mutating commands.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for decisions that materially change the plan, for confirming important assumptions, or for information that cannot be discovered via non-mutating exploration.

## Two kinds of unknowns (treat differently)

1. **Discoverable facts** (repo/system truth): explore first.

   * Before asking, run targeted searches and check likely sources of truth (configs/manifests/entrypoints/schemas/types/constants).
   * Ask only if: multiple plausible candidates; nothing found but you need a missing identifier/context; or ambiguity is actually product intent.
   * If asking, present concrete candidates (paths/service names) + recommend one.
   * Never ask questions you can answer from your environment (e.g., “where is this struct”).

2. **Preferences/tradeoffs** (not discoverable): ask early.

   * These are intent or implementation preferences that cannot be derived from exploration.
   * Provide 2–4 mutually exclusive options + a recommended default.
   * If unanswered, proceed with the recommended option and record it as an assumption in the final plan.

## Finalization rule

Only output the final plan when it is decision complete and leaves no decisions to the implementer.

When you present the official plan, wrap it in a `<proposed_plan>` block so the client can render it specially:

1) The opening tag must be on its own line.
2) Start the plan content on the next line (no text on the same line as the tag).
3) The closing tag must be on its own line.
4) Use Markdown inside the block.
5) Keep the tags exactly as `<proposed_plan>` and `</proposed_plan>` (do not translate or rename them), even if the plan content is in another language.

Example:

<proposed_plan>
plan content
</proposed_plan>

plan content should be human and agent digestible. The final plan must be plan-only, concise by default, and include:

* A clear title
* A brief summary section
* Important changes or additions to public APIs/interfaces/types
* Test cases and scenarios
* Explicit assumptions and defaults chosen where needed

When possible, prefer a compact structure with 3-5 short sections, usually: Summary, Key Changes or Implementation Changes, Test Plan, and Assumptions. Do not include a separate Scope section unless scope boundaries are genuinely important to avoid mistakes.

Prefer grouped implementation bullets by subsystem or behavior over file-by-file inventories. Mention files only when needed to disambiguate a non-obvious change, and avoid naming more than 3 paths unless extra specificity is necessary to prevent mistakes. Prefer behavior-level descriptions over symbol-by-symbol removal lists. For v1 feature-addition plans, do not invent detailed schema, validation, precedence, fallback, or wire-shape policy unless the request establishes it or it is needed to prevent a concrete implementation mistake; prefer the intended capability and minimum interface/behavior changes.

Keep bullets short and avoid explanatory sub-bullets unless they are needed to prevent ambiguity. Prefer the minimum detail needed for implementation safety, not exhaustive coverage. Within each section, compress related changes into a few high-signal bullets and omit branch-by-branch logic, repeated invariants, and long lists of unaffected behavior unless they are necessary to prevent a likely implementation mistake. Avoid repeated repo facts and irrelevant edge-case or rollout detail. For straightforward refactors, keep the plan to a compact summary, key edits, tests, and assumptions. If the user asks for more detail, then expand.

Do not ask "should I proceed?" in the final output. The user can easily switch out of Plan mode and request implementation if you have included a `<proposed_plan>` block in your response. Alternatively, they can decide to stay in Plan mode and continue refining the plan.

Only produce at most one `<proposed_plan>` block per turn, and only when you are presenting a complete spec.

If the user stays in Plan mode and asks for revisions after a prior `<proposed_plan>`, any new `<proposed_plan>` must be a complete replacement.
</collaboration_mode>

===== L5209 [assistant] =====
<proposed_plan>
# `ytx board my-tasks` v1 + groundwork for future `q`

## Summary

Implement a new narrow read primitive in [ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx.py): `ytx board my-tasks`.

The goal is to move the fragile “my tasks on the current board/current sprint” decision tree out of `SKILL.md` and into code. The new command becomes the default agent entrypoint for this use case, returns a compact answer-shaped payload for one board, and removes the need for the model to choose between `scoped-issues` and `board issues`.

This change does **not** implement `q` yet. It prepares for it by stabilizing a first high-value read primitive.

## Implementation Changes

### 1. Public CLI contract

Add a new subcommand:

```bash
ytx board my-tasks
ytx board my-tasks --board 83-2561
ytx board my-tasks --board 'PMA iOS Core'
ytx board my-tasks --active-only
ytx board my-tasks --source strict
```

Contract decisions:

- The command is always “my tasks”; v1 does **not** accept `--mine` or `--assignee`.
- The assignee is always resolved through the existing git-email-localpart flow.
- The command always targets the **current sprint** of the selected board.
- `--source` supports `web|strict`, default `web`.
- `--active-only` filters by `resolved == null`, not by state name.
- `--board` accepts either board id or exact board name.
- If the selected instance has exactly one scoped board and `--board` is omitted, use that board automatically.
- If the selected instance has multiple scoped boards and `--board` is omitted, fail with a clear error that requires `--board`.
- If the selected instance has scoped boards and `--board` is provided, the board must resolve **inside that scope**. Do not silently escape scope.
- If the selected instance has no scoped boards and `--board` is omitted, fail with a clear error telling the caller to pass `--board` or configure scope.
- If the resolved board has no current sprint, fail clearly.

Return a single-board compact payload:

```json
{
  "board_id": "83-2561",
  "board_name": "PMA iOS Core",
  "sprint_id": "84-96818",
  "sprint_name": "Спринт 47",
  "source": "web",
  "assignee": "oparin.ivan3",
  "issue_count": 5,
  "issues": [
    {
      "id": "PMA-21079",
      "summary": "...",
      "state": "In Progress",
      "type": "Epic"
    }
  ]
}
```

Notes:

- `issue_count` is after all filtering.
- Default issue items are compact, not raw.
- Do not return `boards: [...]` from `my-tasks`; that shape stays only on `scoped-issues`.

### 2. Internal behavior and refactor

In [ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx.py):

- Add `resolved` to `AgileService.ISSUE_FIELDS`.
- Extend normalized issue handling so `resolved` is available for internal filtering.
- Add a helper for “active only” filtering based on `resolved is None`.
- Add a helper that resolves the target board for `my-tasks`:
  - single scoped board -> use it
  - multiple scoped boards -> require `--board`
  - scoped + `--board` -> resolve only within scope
  - no scope + `--board` -> resolve globally with existing board lookup
  - no scope + no `--board` -> fail
- Reuse existing `build_board_issues_payload` machinery where sensible, but refactor as needed so `my-tasks` does not need to build the broader `scoped-issues` wrapper first.
- Compute assignee resolution once and expose the resolved login in the top-level `assignee` field.
- Keep `board issues` and `scoped-issues` behavior unchanged; `my-tasks` is an additive specialized command.

### 3. Skill/docs behavior update

Update [SKILL.md](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md) to make `ytx board my-tasks` the default happy path for:
- “какие мои задачи”
- “мои задачи на доске”
- “what are my tasks this sprint”

Document the new behavioral split:

- use `board my-tasks` for direct agent answers
- use `board issues` / `scoped-issues` for lower-level inspection and debugging

Also document:
- multiple scoped boards require `--board`
- `--active-only` is based on `resolved`, not workflow state names

### 4. Deferred next phase: `q`

Do **not** implement `q` in this change.

This change should prepare for it by making `my-tasks` a stable primitive that `q` can wrap later. The intended future direction is:

- `ytx q 'my_tasks() { ... }'`
- `ytx q 'issue(id="...") { ... }'`

but the parser, field registry, schema, and batching layer stay out of scope for this implementation.

## Test Plan

Add or update tests in [test_ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/tests/test_ytx.py):

- Parser accepts `board my-tasks`, `--board`, `--source`, and `--active-only`.
- Single scoped board + no `--board` picks that board automatically.
- Multiple scoped boards + no `--board` fails with the expected error.
- Scoped boards + `--board` resolves only within scope and fails if the requested board is outside scope.
- No scope + explicit `--board` works through normal board resolution.
- No scope + no `--board` fails with the expected guidance.
- `--active-only` filters out issues with non-null `resolved`.
- Default output shape is the single-board answer-shaped payload above.
- `assignee` in the top-level payload is the resolved login from the current developer resolution.
- Existing `board issues` and `board scoped-issues` tests remain green.

Recommended manual smoke tests:

- one-scoped-board instance: `ytx board my-tasks`
- one-scoped-board instance: `ytx board my-tasks --active-only`
- multi-scoped-board instance: `ytx board my-tasks` -> clear error
- multi-scoped-board instance: `ytx board my-tasks --board <id>` -> success
- explicit board name: `ytx board my-tasks --board 'Exact Board Name'`
- `--source strict` on a board known to differ from `web`

## Assumptions And Defaults

- V1 scope is only `ytx board my-tasks`; `q` is explicitly deferred.
- `my-tasks` is current-sprint-only in v1; there is no `--sprint-id`.
- `--active-only` means `resolved == null`.
- Multiple scoped boards without `--board` are treated as an error, not as grouped or flattened output.
- The command stays read-only and compact by default; no raw/debug mode is added in v1.
</proposed_plan>

<skill>
<name>youtrack-cli</name>
<path>/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Default Mode

- Execute the bundled commands yourself and return the result.
- Do not answer with a shell tutorial when the skill is already installed and authentication exists.
- Show commands only when the user explicitly asks for instructions or when setup/auth is missing.
- Answer in the user's language unless the user explicitly asks for a different language.
- Do not repeat an identical successful read command. Reuse the first successful result unless the context changed or the first result was incomplete.
- Before sending the final answer, do one completeness check against the original user request. If the answer is not good enough yet, keep using tools instead of finalizing.
- Resolve command paths from this skill file path.
- If the skill file path is `/abs/path/to/SKILL.md`, then:
  - `<yt-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/yt`
  - `<ytx-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/ytx`
- Use those absolute command paths for every command in this skill.
- Do not run `scripts/yt` or `scripts/ytx` as paths relative to the current working directory.
- Do not use a bare `yt` from `PATH`.

## Resolve Context First

Always begin with these two commands in this order:

```bash
<yt-command> instances list
<yt-command> instances current
```

Use the result to choose the instance context:

- If the user names a label and it exists, use `--instance <label>` for this turn.
- If `instances current` already points at the correct label, prefer commands without `--instance`.
- If several labels exist and there is no active instance, ask which label to use.
- If no labels exist, stop and ask the user to log in first.

In all commands below:

- `<label>` is a real label from `<yt-command> instances list`
- `<board-id>` is a real agile board id
- `<issue-id>` is a real issue id

## Fast Path: My Tasks

For prompts like:

- "какие мои задачи"
- "мои задачи на доске"
- "what are my tasks this sprint"

use this order:

1. Run `<yt-command> instances list`
2. Run `<yt-command> instances current`
3. If the user asks about current sprint or "my tasks" without naming a board, and the selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, run:

```bash
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

4. If the user asked about a specific board, use board-specific reads for the final answer. When the selected instance has scoped boards, identify the board inside that scope first:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web
```

5. Only if there is no scope, broaden to:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list
```

Return the actual tasks. Do not ask the user to run these commands unless the skill is not installed or auth is missing.

If the selected instance has exactly one scoped board and the user refers to "the board" loosely, treat that single scoped board as the target board.

## Scope Rules For Large Instances

- If `scoped_board_ids` is configured for the selected instance, treat that scope as the default search surface.
- Do not start with unrestricted `<ytx-command> board list` on large instances.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for developer-focused reads.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board list --scoped` before any full-instance board enumeration.

## Board Reads

Use `<ytx-command>` for agent-facing reads:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board show <board-id>
<ytx-command> board sprints <board-id> --current
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --source web
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--source web` when the user asks what is visible on the board.
- Use the strict sprint endpoint only when the user explicitly asks about sprint membership.
- Use `--mine` when the request is about the current developer.
- For a board-specific question, prefer `<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web` over `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for the final answer.

## Issue Reads

```bash
<ytx-command> issue show <issue-id>
<ytx-command> issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
<ytx-command> issue comment-list <issue-id>
```

## Mutations

Use the wrappers directly for common writes:

```bash
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
<ytx-command> issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Comment text'
<ytx-command> issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
<ytx-command> issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
<ytx-command> issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important' --dry-run
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- For board membership on explicit boards, use `board-add` and `board-remove` instead of guessing field updates.

## Current Developer Resolution

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value.

If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with candidate users. In that case, ask the user which account to use.

## Ask Only If Blocked

Ask the user only when one of these is true:

- no YouTrack instances are configured
- the requested label does not exist
- several labels exist and there is no active instance
- no scoped board matches the request and the target board cannot be identified
- a write target is missing or ambiguous

Otherwise, run the commands and return the result.

## Output Contract

When answering a read query, return the result itself, not a command recipe.

For task lists on a board or sprint:

- include board name
- include sprint name when available
- include total matching issue count
- list every matching issue id, summary, state, and type
- do not omit `Done` issues unless the user explicitly asks for only active or unresolved work
- write the final answer in the user's language

For a single issue:

- include id, summary, state, type, priority, assignee, and the main description

## Pre-final Check

Before sending the final answer:

1. Draft the answer from the tool results you already have.
2. Compare that draft against the original user request.
3. Ask these questions:
   - Does the draft answer the user's actual question directly?
   - If the user named a specific instance, board, sprint, or issue, does the answer refer to that exact target?
   - Does the answer satisfy the output contract for this query type?
   - Is the answer still telling the user to run commands even though the skill and auth are already available?
4. If any answer is "no", continue with the next needed tool call instead of finalizing.
5. If all answers are "yes", send that draft as the final answer.

Do this once per response. Do not loop on repeated self-checks after you already have a good direct answer.

## Setup Fallback

Use this only when installation or authentication is missing.

Install globally:

```bash
setup.sh global --locale <locale>
```

Install into one repository:

```bash
setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale <locale>
```

Log in with an explicit label and optional scoped boards:

```bash
<yt-command> \
  --instance <label> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

Pin the active instance:

```bash
<yt-command> instances use <label>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain through `<yt-command> auth login`.
- Do not save tokens in shell history, env files, or checked-in files.
- Use an explicit label for each instance.
- On large instances, keep agents inside scoped boards by default.
- Prefer `<ytx-command>` for reads consumed by agents because it emits stable JSON.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

</skill>

# `ytx board my-tasks` + TSV output + clickable YouTrack links

## Summary

Implement `ytx board my-tasks` as the primary agent-facing read for “my tasks on this board / this sprint”, and pair it with a stricter presentation contract in the skill.

This change has two goals:

- move board/scope/sprint/assignee decisions out of `SKILL.md` and into `ytx`
- make final answers more terminal-friendly by default: stable TSV-style output in a fenced block and full clickable issue URLs for every row

This is the next step before any `q` layer. `q` stays deferred.

## Key Changes

### 1. Add `ytx board my-tasks`

Implement a new board subcommand in [ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx.py):

```bash
ytx board my-tasks
ytx board my-tasks --board 83-2561
ytx board my-tasks --board 'PMA iOS Core'
ytx board my-tasks --active-only
ytx board my-tasks --source strict
```

Behavior:

- Always resolve instance through the existing runtime.
- Always resolve the current developer through the existing `--mine` logic internally. V1 does not expose `--mine` or `--assignee`; the command is specifically “my tasks”.
- Default to `source=web`.
- Always target the current sprint of the selected board.
- `--active-only` means `resolved == null`, not “state != Done”.
- If the selected instance has exactly one scoped board and `--board` is omitted, use that board automatically.
- If the selected instance has multiple scoped boards and `--board` is omitted, fail and require `--board`.
- If scoped boards exist and `--board` is provided, resolve only inside that scope.
- If no scoped boards exist and `--board` is omitted, fail clearly.
- If the board has no current sprint, fail clearly.

Return a single-board, answer-shaped payload:

```json
{
  "board_id": "83-2561",
  "board_name": "PMA iOS Core",
  "board_url": "https://host/agiles/83-2561/current",
  "sprint_id": "84-96818",
  "sprint_name": "Спринт 47",
  "source": "web",
  "assignee": "oparin.ivan3",
  "issue_count": 5,
  "issues": [
    {
      "id": "PMA-21079",
      "url": "https://host/issue/PMA-21079",
      "summary": "...",
      "state": "In Progress",
      "type": "Epic"
    }
  ]
}
```

Decisions:
- `my-tasks` returns one board only.
- It does not return `boards: [...]`.
- It includes URLs in the payload so the model does not need to construct them itself.

### 2. Add URL fields to read payloads

Use `base_url` from the selected instance record in [instance_runtime.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/instance_runtime.py) to build stable clickable links.

Add helper logic for:

- `issue_url = {base_url.rstrip('/')}/issue/{id}`
- `board_url = {base_url.rstrip('/')}/agiles/{board_id}/current` for current-sprint board views

Apply this to `my-tasks` first. Also add non-breaking `url` enrichment to existing normalized single-issue reads where practical:

- `ytx issue show`
- `ytx issue search`
- existing board issue payloads may also include `url` on each issue if that does not complicate compatibility

Graceful fallback:
- if `base_url` is missing for the selected instance, omit URL fields rather than failing

### 3. Tighten the skill output contract for terminal UX

Update [SKILL.md](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md) so that list responses use a fenced TSV-style block by default instead of Markdown tables or loose bullets.

For board/sprint task lists:

- header prose above the block:
  - board name
  - board URL when available
  - sprint name
  - total issue count
- then a fenced code block with literal tab-separated columns
- default column order:
  - `Issue`
  - `State`
  - `Type`
  - `Link`
  - `Summary`

Example shape:

```text
Board: PMA iOS Core
Board URL: https://host/agiles/83-2561/current
Sprint: Спринт 47
Total: 5

Issue	State	Type	Link	Summary
PMA-21079	In Progress	Epic	https://host/issue/PMA-21079	[iOS] Проработка инфраструктуры агентского кодинга
```

Rules:

- full raw URLs, not Markdown links, so terminal clickability works reliably
- no Markdown table for multi-row task lists
- no omission of `Done` issues unless the user explicitly asks for active/unresolved only
- single-issue answers should include a dedicated `Link:` line near the top

### 4. Update the skill happy path

Revise the skill instructions so the default path becomes:

- use `ytx board my-tasks` for “my tasks / my sprint / my board”
- use `ytx board issues` and `ytx board scoped-issues` only for debugging or lower-level inspection
- when the user asks about a specific board, prefer `my-tasks --board ...`
- when the user wants active work only, prefer `my-tasks --active-only`

Document explicitly that the final answer should use the TSV contract and include clickable links.

## Test Plan

Add/extend tests in [test_ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/tests/test_ytx.py):

- parser accepts `board my-tasks`, `--board`, `--active-only`, `--source`
- one scoped board + no `--board` uses that board
- multiple scoped boards + no `--board` fails with the expected error
- scoped boards + `--board` resolve only within scope
- no scope + no `--board` fails clearly
- `--active-only` filters by `resolved == null`
- `my-tasks` returns the single-board payload shape above
- `my-tasks` includes top-level `board_url` and per-issue `url` when `base_url` exists
- URL fields are omitted cleanly when `base_url` is absent
- existing board/issue commands remain green

Manual validation:

- ask the weak model: “посмотри мои задачи на wb доске”
- verify it uses `my-tasks` instead of `scoped-issues` + post-processing
- verify final answer is in Russian
- verify output is a TSV-style code block, not bullets or a broken Markdown table
- verify each task row includes a clickable full URL
- verify single-issue request includes a direct issue URL

## Assumptions

- `my-tasks` is the next implementation step; `q` stays out of scope for this change.
- `--active-only` is defined by `resolved`, not workflow state names.
- Multiple scoped boards without `--board` are treated as an error, not grouped or flattened output.
- The preferred terminal presentation is a fenced TSV-style block with full raw URLs in every task row.
- `board_url` should point to the current agile board view: `/agiles/<board_id>/current`.
</proposed_plan>

# Clickable YouTrack Issue Links

## Summary

Add first-class clickable issue links to the `youtrack-cli` skill by enriching `ytx` read payloads with full issue URLs derived from the selected instance `base_url`.

This change is intentionally narrow:
- add issue URLs to `ytx` read outputs
- update the skill contract so agents include raw full URLs in final answers
- do **not** change the broader task-list formatting yet
- do **not** implement `my-tasks` or `q` in this change

## Key Changes

### 1. Add URL enrichment to normalized issue payloads

In [ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/ytx.py), add helper logic that builds:

```text
{base_url.rstrip('/')}/issue/{issue_id}
```

Use the selected instance `base_url` from [instance_runtime.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/scripts/instance_runtime.py).

Apply this to normalized issue outputs so every normalized issue may include:

```json
{
  "id": "PMA-21079",
  "url": "https://youtrack.example.com/issue/PMA-21079",
  "summary": "...",
  "state": "In Progress",
  "type": "Epic"
}
```

Recommended implementation shape:
- add a small helper like `issue_url(base_url, issue_id)`
- thread `base_url` into `normalize_issue(...)`
- keep URL generation centralized and string-safe
- if `base_url` or `issue_id` is missing, omit `url` instead of failing

### 2. Add links to existing read surfaces

Add `url` to the existing `ytx` read commands that return normalized issues:

- `ytx issue show`
- `ytx issue search`
- `ytx board issues`
- `ytx board scoped-issues`

Behavior:
- single-issue reads get one `url`
- issue lists get `url` on every issue row
- raw mode stays raw; do not rewrite raw upstream payloads
- non-issue payloads do not need link enrichment in this change

Do **not** add board URLs in this change. Keep scope to clickable issue links only.

### 3. Keep runtime lookup simple and local

Use the already available selected instance record from the current install/runtime context.

Recommended minimal integration:
- add a small helper in `ytx.py` to read the selected instance record once per request
- extract `base_url` from the resolved instance config/runtime
- pass that `base_url` only into the issue-normalization path

Do not redesign the runtime layer for this.
Do not add a new CLI command for links.

### 4. Update the skill answer contract

Update [SKILL.md](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md) so that when the agent answers with tasks or a single issue, it should include the full raw issue URL from the tool payload.

Rules:
- use raw full URLs, not Markdown links
- include one URL per issue when listing tasks
- include a dedicated `Link:` line near the top for single-issue answers
- do not ask the model to construct URLs manually if the payload already contains them

Formatting changes beyond this are deferred. No TSV or table policy change in this plan.

## Test Plan

Add or update tests in [test_ytx.py](/Users/iv/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/tests/test_ytx.py):

- `normalize_issue` includes `url` when both `base_url` and readable issue id exist
- `normalize_issue` omits `url` when `base_url` is missing
- `issue show` returns normalized payload with `url`
- `issue search` returns normalized list with `url` on each issue
- `board issues` returns `url` on each issue
- `board scoped-issues` returns `url` on each issue in every board payload
- raw mode does not inject normalized `url` fields into raw upstream payloads

Manual validation:
- run `ytx issue show <issue-id>` and verify the payload contains a full issue URL
- run `ytx board issues <board-id> --source web` and verify every issue has a clickable URL
- ask the agent for a task list and verify the final answer includes full raw URLs that can be clicked from the terminal

## Assumptions

- This plan covers only clickable issue links, not board links.
- This plan does not introduce new commands.
- This plan does not change the default formatting contract beyond “include full raw issue URLs in final answers”.
- URL generation should depend only on the selected instance `base_url` and the readable issue id.
</proposed_plan>

<skill>
<name>youtrack-cli</name>
<path>/Users/iv/.local/share/agents/skills/youtrack-cli/SKILL.md</path>
---
name: youtrack-cli
description: "Используй, когда нужно работать с одним или несколькими self-hosted или cloud YouTrack инстансами через локальный CLI вместо MCP. Поддерживаются именованные labels инстансов, авторизация через macOS Keychain, optional scoped board ids для больших инстансов, просмотр досок и спринтов, поиск задач, комментарии, обновление полей, явное управление board/sprint membership и agent-facing JSON reads через bundled `ytx` wrapper."
---

# YouTrack CLI

## Default Mode

- Execute the bundled commands yourself and return the result.
- Do not answer with a shell tutorial when the skill is already installed and authentication exists.
- Show commands only when the user explicitly asks for instructions or when setup/auth is missing.
- Answer in the user's language unless the user explicitly asks for a different language.
- Do not repeat an identical successful read command. Reuse the first successful result unless the context changed or the first result was incomplete.
- Before sending the final answer, do one completeness check against the original user request. If the answer is not good enough yet, keep using tools instead of finalizing.
- Resolve command paths from this skill file path.
- If the skill file path is `/abs/path/to/SKILL.md`, then:
  - `<yt-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/yt`
  - `<ytx-command>` is `/abs/path/to/scripts/ytx`
- Use those absolute command paths for every command in this skill.
- Do not run `scripts/yt` or `scripts/ytx` as paths relative to the current working directory.
- Do not use a bare `yt` from `PATH`.

## Resolve Context First

Always begin with these two commands in this order:

```bash
<yt-command> instances list
<yt-command> instances current
```

Use the result to choose the instance context:

- If the user names a label and it exists, use `--instance <label>` for this turn.
- If `instances current` already points at the correct label, prefer commands without `--instance`.
- If several labels exist and there is no active instance, ask which label to use.
- If no labels exist, stop and ask the user to log in first.

In all commands below:

- `<label>` is a real label from `<yt-command> instances list`
- `<board-id>` is a real agile board id
- `<issue-id>` is a real issue id

## Fast Path: My Tasks

For prompts like:

- "какие мои задачи"
- "мои задачи на доске"
- "what are my tasks this sprint"

use this order:

1. Run `<yt-command> instances list`
2. Run `<yt-command> instances current`
3. If the user asks about current sprint or "my tasks" without naming a board, and the selected instance has `scoped_board_ids`, run:

```bash
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

4. If the user asked about a specific board, use board-specific reads for the final answer. When the selected instance has scoped boards, identify the board inside that scope first:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web
```

5. Only if there is no scope, broaden to:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list
```

Return the actual tasks. Do not ask the user to run these commands unless the skill is not installed or auth is missing.

If the selected instance has exactly one scoped board and the user refers to "the board" loosely, treat that single scoped board as the target board.

## Scope Rules For Large Instances

- If `scoped_board_ids` is configured for the selected instance, treat that scope as the default search surface.
- Do not start with unrestricted `<ytx-command> board list` on large instances.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for developer-focused reads.
- Prefer `<ytx-command> board list --scoped` before any full-instance board enumeration.

## Board Reads

Use `<ytx-command>` for agent-facing reads:

```bash
<ytx-command> board list --scoped
<ytx-command> board show <board-id>
<ytx-command> board sprints <board-id> --current
<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --source web
<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--source web` when the user asks what is visible on the board.
- Use the strict sprint endpoint only when the user explicitly asks about sprint membership.
- Use `--mine` when the request is about the current developer.
- For a board-specific question, prefer `<ytx-command> board issues <board-id> --mine --source web` over `<ytx-command> board scoped-issues --mine` for the final answer.

## Issue Reads

```bash
<ytx-command> issue show <issue-id>
<ytx-command> issue search 'id: <issue-id>'
<ytx-command> issue comment-list <issue-id>
```

## Mutations

Use the wrappers directly for common writes:

```bash
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --state 'In Progress'
<ytx-command> issue update <issue-id> --custom-field 'Field Name=Value'
<ytx-command> issue comment-add <issue-id> 'Comment text'
<ytx-command> issue board-add <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --current-sprint
<ytx-command> issue board-remove <issue-id> --board '<board-name>' --sprint '<sprint-name>'
<ytx-command> issue command <issue-id> 'add tag important' --dry-run
```

Rules:

- Prefer `--dry-run` before raw command mutations when the effect is not obvious.
- For board membership on explicit boards, use `board-add` and `board-remove` instead of guessing field updates.

## Current Developer Resolution

`--mine` resolves the current developer from `git config user.email`, falls back to global git config, takes the localpart before `@`, and searches YouTrack users with that value.

If the match is ambiguous, `ytx` fails with candidate users. In that case, ask the user which account to use.

## Ask Only If Blocked

Ask the user only when one of these is true:

- no YouTrack instances are configured
- the requested label does not exist
- several labels exist and there is no active instance
- no scoped board matches the request and the target board cannot be identified
- a write target is missing or ambiguous

Otherwise, run the commands and return the result.

## Output Contract

When answering a read query, return the result itself, not a command recipe.

For task lists on a board or sprint:

- include board name
- include sprint name when available
- include total matching issue count
- list every matching issue id, summary, state, and type
- include the full raw issue URL for every listed issue when the tool payload provides it
- do not omit `Done` issues unless the user explicitly asks for only active or unresolved work
- write the final answer in the user's language

For a single issue:

- include id, summary, state, type, priority, assignee, and the main description
- include a dedicated `Link:` line near the top when the tool payload provides a full issue URL

## Pre-final Check

Before sending the final answer:

1. Draft the answer from the tool results you already have.
2. Compare that draft against the original user request.
3. Ask these questions:
   - Does the draft answer the user's actual question directly?
   - If the user named a specific instance, board, sprint, or issue, does the answer refer to that exact target?
   - Does the answer satisfy the output contract for this query type?
   - Is the answer still telling the user to run commands even though the skill and auth are already available?
4. If any answer is "no", continue with the next needed tool call instead of finalizing.
5. If all answers are "yes", send that draft as the final answer.

Do this once per response. Do not loop on repeated self-checks after you already have a good direct answer.

## Setup Fallback

Use this only when installation or authentication is missing.

Install globally:

```bash
setup.sh global --locale <locale>
```

Install into one repository:

```bash
setup.sh local /abs/path/to/repo --locale <locale>
```

Log in with an explicit label and optional scoped boards:

```bash
<yt-command> \
  --instance <label> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  --board-id <board-id> \
  auth login \
  --base-url https://your-youtrack-host
```

Pin the active instance:

```bash
<yt-command> instances use <label>
```

## Safety Rules

- Keep tokens in macOS Keychain through `<yt-command> auth login`.
- Do not save tokens in shell history, env files, or checked-in files.
- Use an explicit label for each instance.
- On large instances, keep agents inside scoped boards by default.
- Prefer `<ytx-command>` for reads consumed by agents because it emits stable JSON.

## References

- [`references/board-membership.md`](references/board-membership.md) for explicit/manual board behavior and `web` vs `strict` reads

</skill>
