<proposed_plan>
# Weak-Model-Safe `AGENTS.md` Refactor for WBPartners

## Summary

- Перестроить root `AGENTS.md` из handbook-style файла в короткий operational contract для слабых моделей.
- Вынести подробности в несколько узких agent-facing docs под `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`.
- Оставить существующий `CLAUDE.md` как symlink на `AGENTS.md`; не создавать второй источник правды.
- Этот проход только documentation: без новых скриптов, `make`-таргетов и без автоматизации worktree/session handoff.

## Key Changes

### 1. Переписать root `AGENTS.md` как task-first контракт
Новая структура root файла:

- `Purpose`
- `Default Mode`
- `Language Policy`
- `Task Modes`
- `New Task Entry`
- `Active Task Execution`
- `Core Invariants`
- `Required Validation`
- `Ask Only If Blocked`
- `Reference Map`

Обязательные правила в root:

- Если агент не находится уже в правильном dedicated worktree для задачи, он не должен начинать кодить из shared checkout.
- Правильный вход в задачу:
  - синхронизировать `develop`
  - получить свои задачи через `$youtrack-cli` (`mine`)
  - если задача не указана явно, спросить пользователя, какую брать
  - создать worktree от `develop`
  - создать branch по `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md`
  - запушить branch, назначить upstream, создать Draft MR
  - вывести handoff-команды:
    - `Ctrl+C`
    - `cd <worktree-path>`
    - `codex resume <session-id>` если session id виден
    - иначе обычный запуск Codex в worktree
- `make project` / `make device` не описывать как первый шаг входа в задачу.
  Эти команды должны фигурировать как bootstrap после перехода в worktree, когда генерация действительно нужна.
- Root должен быть balanced по размеру: короткий, но не телеграфный.
- В каждом крупном разделе root должен быть явный reference на релевантный файл из `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`.

Также в этом проходе:
- исправить заголовок `# CLAUDE.md` на `# AGENTS.md`
- сохранить слабомодельный стиль:
  - короткие директивные секции
  - один preferred path на задачу
  - минимальная свобода интерпретации
  - без tutorial-style prose

### 2. Добавить новый слой docs в `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`
Создать следующие документы.

`TASK_ENTRY_WORKFLOW.md`
- Source of truth для входа в задачу.
- Содержит точный пошаговый workflow:
  - проверить, не в task worktree ли уже агент
  - `develop` -> `pull`
  - `$youtrack-cli` -> `mine`
  - выбор тикета
  - worktree path по gitflow-договорённости
  - branch naming по `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md`
  - push + upstream
  - Draft MR через `glab`
  - session handoff
- Отдельно зафиксировать текущее ограничение:
  - автоматическое извлечение session id пока не обещается
  - если id виден в текущем Codex footer, использовать его
  - если нет, явно сказать пользователю перезапустить Codex уже внутри worktree
- Отдельно зафиксировать, когда после входа в worktree запускать `make project` / `make device`.

`VALIDATION_AND_GATES.md`
- Source of truth для обязательных локальных проверок перед завершением работы.
- Явно перечислить команды:
  - `make check`
  - `make clean-imports`
  - `mise run lint:stream <current-stream>`
  - `mise run periphery:changes`
  - `xcodebuild -workspace WBPartners.xcworkspace -scheme WBPartners -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build`
- `mise run periphery:stream <current-stream>` описать как обязательный escalation gate для:
  - shared/core changes
  - cross-stream changes
  - крупных рефакторингов
- Добавить правило про targeted tests:
  - unit/UI tests запускаются по форме изменения
  - test plan source of truth: `App/UnitTests.xctestplan`
- Явно зафиксировать:
  - stream name нельзя угадывать
  - его нужно брать из `STREAMS.md`
  - если mapping ещё не определён, спрашивать пользователя

`ARCHITECTURE_RULES.md`
- Сжатый agent-facing summary архитектурных инвариантов:
  - module types
  - interface-only deps
  - Utility exception
  - роли View / ViewModel / Interactor / Service / Mapper / Models
  - no unnecessary layers
  - no direct module-to-module deps
- Не дублировать длинные объяснения.
- В конце ссылаться на существующую DocC architecture документацию как deep reference.

`TESTING_RULES.md`
- Сжатый agent-facing summary тестовых правил:
  - business logic требует tests
  - тестировать Interactor / Service / Mapper
  - никогда не unit-test ViewModel
  - `sut`
  - Arrange / Act / Assert
  - Act = single method call
  - black-box only
  - test classes `final`
- В конце ссылаться на существующие DocC `UnitTesting` и `UITesting`.

`STREAMS.md`
- Ввести как отдельный agent-facing source of truth для:
  - списка stream names
  - способа резолва current developer stream
  - правил, когда использовать `lint:stream` и `periphery:stream`
- Так как сейчас такого source of truth ещё нет, в v1 документ должен явно содержать fallback:
  - если current-user stream не может быть однозначно определён из будущего конфига, агент не гадает и спрашивает пользователя

### 3. Зафиксировать границы между root, AgenticDocs и существующей DocC
Слой обязанностей должен быть таким:

- Root `AGENTS.md`
  - только operational contract и decision order
- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/*`
  - agent-optimized reference layer
- `App/Documentation/Documentation.docc/*`
  - глубокая человеческая документация и background material

Правила migration:

- Из текущего большого `AGENTS.md` вынести:
  - `Build Commands`, `Linting & Code Quality`, `Tool Versions`, `Code Generation`, локальные validation ожидания -> `VALIDATION_AND_GATES.md`
  - `Architecture`, `Dependency Rules`, `Conventions` -> `ARCHITECTURE_RULES.md`
  - `Testing` -> `TESTING_RULES.md`
- `Naming & Workflow` оставить anchored в существующем `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md`
- `TASK_ENTRY_WORKFLOW.md` сделать operational doc поверх `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md`, не дублируя туда naming rules второй раз
- При необходимости добавить взаимные cross-links:
  - из root в AgenticDocs
  - из AgenticDocs в DocC
  - из `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md` в `TASK_ENTRY_WORKFLOW.md`

### 4. Стиль написания всех новых agent docs
Для root и всех новых файлов в `AgenticDocs` использовать один и тот же weak-model-safe стиль:

- английский язык для agentic docs
- короткие директивные секции
- один happy path на интент
- fallback только после preferred path
- явный `Ask Only If Blocked`
- явный `Output/Action Contract`
- без длинных narrative explanations
- без mixed-priority handbook структуры

## Test Plan

- Прочитать новый root `AGENTS.md` изолированно и проверить, что агент может понять:
  - как войти в новую задачу
  - как продолжить работу в уже существующем worktree
  - какие валидации обязательны
  без открытия других файлов до момента, когда нужен detail
- Проверить, что каждый крупный раздел root имеет один явный reference в `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`.
- Прогнать mental dry-run по типичным слабомодельным запросам:
  - “возьми следующую задачу”
  - “начни PMA-xxxxx”
  - “исправь баг в текущей задаче”
  - “подготовь MR”
  и убедиться, что модель сначала уходит в worktree/task-entry flow, а не начинает кодить в shared checkout
- Проверить, что validation section толкает к конкретным repo-gates, а не к общему “run lint/tests”.
- Проверить, что `STREAMS.md` не оставляет серой зоны:
  - либо есть способ определить stream
  - либо есть явное правило спросить пользователя
- Проверить отсутствие split-brain:
  - naming/path rules живут в `GITFLOW_CONVENTIONS.md`
  - task-entry commands живут в `TASK_ENTRY_WORKFLOW.md`
  - глубокая архитектура и тестовые handbook’и остаются в DocC

## Assumptions

- Этот проход ограничен документацией.
- `CLAUDE.md` остаётся symlink на root `AGENTS.md`.
- Agentic docs в `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/` остаются на английском, как часть agentic instruction layer.
- Канонический simulator build gate в новой validation doc закрепляется через `WBPartners.xcworkspace`, scheme `WBPartners` и generic iOS Simulator destination.
- Автоматическое извлечение session id и автоматический worktree bootstrap не входят в этот проход; документация должна описывать текущий manual handoff честно и без обещания несуществующей автоматизации.
</proposed_plan>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

The `request_user_input` tool is unavailable in Default mode. If you call it while in Default mode, it will return an error.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions. If you absolutely must ask a question because the answer cannot be discovered from local context and a reasonable assumption would be risky, ask the user directly with a concise plain-text question. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

- `TASK_ENTRY_WORKFLOW.md`

===== L6885 [developer] =====
<collaboration_mode># Plan Mode (Conversational)

You work in 3 phases, and you should *chat your way* to a great plan before finalizing it. A great plan is very detailed—intent- and implementation-wise—so that it can be handed to another engineer or agent to be implemented right away. It must be **decision complete**, where the implementer does not need to make any decisions.

## Mode rules (strict)

You are in **Plan Mode** until a developer message explicitly ends it.

Plan Mode is not changed by user intent, tone, or imperative language. If a user asks for execution while still in Plan Mode, treat it as a request to **plan the execution**, not perform it.

## Plan Mode vs update_plan tool

Plan Mode is a collaboration mode that can involve requesting user input and eventually issuing a `<proposed_plan>` block.

Separately, `update_plan` is a checklist/progress/TODOs tool; it does not enter or exit Plan Mode. Do not confuse it with Plan mode or try to use it while in Plan mode. If you try to use `update_plan` in Plan mode, it will return an error.

## Execution vs. mutation in Plan Mode

You may explore and execute **non-mutating** actions that improve the plan. You must not perform **mutating** actions.

### Allowed (non-mutating, plan-improving)

Actions that gather truth, reduce ambiguity, or validate feasibility without changing repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Reading or searching files, configs, schemas, types, manifests, and docs
* Static analysis, inspection, and repo exploration
* Dry-run style commands when they do not edit repo-tracked files
* Tests, builds, or checks that may write to caches or build artifacts (for example, `target/`, `.cache/`, or snapshots) so long as they do not edit repo-tracked files

### Not allowed (mutating, plan-executing)

Actions that implement the plan or change repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Editing or writing files
* Running formatters or linters that rewrite files
* Applying patches, migrations, or codegen that updates repo-tracked files
* Side-effectful commands whose purpose is to carry out the plan rather than refine it

When in doubt: if the action would reasonably be described as "doing the work" rather than "planning the work," do not do it.

## PHASE 1 — Ground in the environment (explore first, ask second)

Begin by grounding yourself in the actual environment. Eliminate unknowns in the prompt by discovering facts, not by asking the user. Resolve all questions that can be answered through exploration or inspection. Identify missing or ambiguous details only if they cannot be derived from the environment. Silent exploration between turns is allowed and encouraged.

Before asking the user any question, perform at least one targeted non-mutating exploration pass (for example: search relevant files, inspect likely entrypoints/configs, confirm current implementation shape), unless no local environment/repo is available.

Exception: you may ask clarifying questions about the user's prompt before exploring, ONLY if there are obvious ambiguities or contradictions in the prompt itself. However, if ambiguity might be resolved by exploring, always prefer exploring first.

Do not ask questions that can be answered from the repo or system (for example, "where is this struct?" or "which UI component should we use?" when exploration can make it clear). Only ask once you have exhausted reasonable non-mutating exploration.

## PHASE 2 — Intent chat (what they actually want)

* Keep asking until you can clearly state: goal + success criteria, audience, in/out of scope, constraints, current state, and the key preferences/tradeoffs.
* Bias toward questions over guessing: if any high-impact ambiguity remains, do NOT plan yet—ask.

## PHASE 3 — Implementation chat (what/how we’ll build)

* Once intent is stable, keep asking until the spec is decision complete: approach, interfaces (APIs/schemas/I/O), data flow, edge cases/failure modes, testing + acceptance criteria, rollout/monitoring, and any migrations/compat constraints.

## Asking questions

Critical rules:

* Strongly prefer using the `request_user_input` tool to ask any questions.
* Offer only meaningful multiple‑choice options; don’t include filler choices that are obviously wrong or irrelevant.
* In rare cases where an unavoidable, important question can’t be expressed with reasonable multiple‑choice options (due to extreme ambiguity), you may ask it directly without the tool.

You SHOULD ask many questions, but each question must:

* materially change the spec/plan, OR
* confirm/lock an assumption, OR
* choose between meaningful tradeoffs.
* not be answerable by non-mutating commands.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for decisions that materially change the plan, for confirming important assumptions, or for information that cannot be discovered via non-mutating exploration.

## Two kinds of unknowns (treat differently)

1. **Discoverable facts** (repo/system truth): explore first.

   * Before asking, run targeted searches and check likely sources of truth (configs/manifests/entrypoints/schemas/types/constants).
   * Ask only if: multiple plausible candidates; nothing found but you need a missing identifier/context; or ambiguity is actually product intent.
   * If asking, present concrete candidates (paths/service names) + recommend one.
   * Never ask questions you can answer from your environment (e.g., “where is this struct”).

2. **Preferences/tradeoffs** (not discoverable): ask early.

   * These are intent or implementation preferences that cannot be derived from exploration.
   * Provide 2–4 mutually exclusive options + a recommended default.
   * If unanswered, proceed with the recommended option and record it as an assumption in the final plan.

## Finalization rule

Only output the final plan when it is decision complete and leaves no decisions to the implementer.

When you present the official plan, wrap it in a `<proposed_plan>` block so the client can render it specially:

1) The opening tag must be on its own line.
2) Start the plan content on the next line (no text on the same line as the tag).
3) The closing tag must be on its own line.
4) Use Markdown inside the block.
5) Keep the tags exactly as `<proposed_plan>` and `</proposed_plan>` (do not translate or rename them), even if the plan content is in another language.

Example:

<proposed_plan>
plan content
</proposed_plan>

plan content should be human and agent digestible. The final plan must be plan-only, concise by default, and include:

* A clear title
* A brief summary section
* Important changes or additions to public APIs/interfaces/types
* Test cases and scenarios
* Explicit assumptions and defaults chosen where needed

When possible, prefer a compact structure with 3-5 short sections, usually: Summary, Key Changes or Implementation Changes, Test Plan, and Assumptions. Do not include a separate Scope section unless scope boundaries are genuinely important to avoid mistakes.

Prefer grouped implementation bullets by subsystem or behavior over file-by-file inventories. Mention files only when needed to disambiguate a non-obvious change, and avoid naming more than 3 paths unless extra specificity is necessary to prevent mistakes. Prefer behavior-level descriptions over symbol-by-symbol removal lists. For v1 feature-addition plans, do not invent detailed schema, validation, precedence, fallback, or wire-shape policy unless the request establishes it or it is needed to prevent a concrete implementation mistake; prefer the intended capability and minimum interface/behavior changes.

Keep bullets short and avoid explanatory sub-bullets unless they are needed to prevent ambiguity. Prefer the minimum detail needed for implementation safety, not exhaustive coverage. Within each section, compress related changes into a few high-signal bullets and omit branch-by-branch logic, repeated invariants, and long lists of unaffected behavior unless they are necessary to prevent a likely implementation mistake. Avoid repeated repo facts and irrelevant edge-case or rollout detail. For straightforward refactors, keep the plan to a compact summary, key edits, tests, and assumptions. If the user asks for more detail, then expand.

Do not ask "should I proceed?" in the final output. The user can easily switch out of Plan mode and request implementation if you have included a `<proposed_plan>` block in your response. Alternatively, they can decide to stay in Plan mode and continue refining the plan.

Only produce at most one `<proposed_plan>` block per turn, and only when you are presenting a complete spec.

If the user stays in Plan mode and asks for revisions after a prior `<proposed_plan>`, any new `<proposed_plan>` must be a complete replacement.
</collaboration_mode>

# Добавить Product Context Layer в `AGENTS.md` и `AgenticDocs`

## Summary

Поднять из product-forensics research только устойчивый, высокосигнальный продуктовый контекст, который помогает агенту быстрее понимать код и не делать неверных предположений о shell, auth, supplier context и gating.

Сделать это в два слоя:

- короткий `Product Context` блок в root `AGENTS.md`
- два новых on-demand reference docs в `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`

При этом:

- не тащить в root длинные исследования, метрики и deep-dive prose
- не использовать `@`-include для новых файлов
- не делать финальные agent docs зависимыми от sibling checkout paths из `/Users/iv/Developer/Wildberries/partners-app-dev/.research/...`

## Key Changes

### 1. Добавить `Product Context` в root `AGENTS.md`

Добавить новый короткий раздел сразу после `Purpose` или сразу перед `Task Modes`.

В этот раздел поднять только 5 устойчивых инвариантов:

- `WBPartners` — это iOS seller app для Wildberries, не consumer app.
- Базовая продуктовая модель — `supplier-scoped seller workspace`.
- После auth app не просто “логинит”, а восстанавливает или резолвит supplier context, и только потом открывает рабочее пространство.
- User-visible behavior часто определяется комбинацией:
  - selected supplier
  - KVS remote config
  - ABAC permissions
  - deeplink / push entry path
- Shell и routing находятся в migration: legacy и new flows coexist, поэтому нельзя предполагать один единственный navigation path.

В конце раздела дать две явные ссылки:
- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`
- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

Также в `Active Task Execution` добавить короткое правило:
- если задача касается auth, root shell, tabs, dashboard, deeplinks, supplier switching или feature gating, открыть `SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md` до редактирования

### 2. Добавить `PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`

Создать новый файл:
- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`

Это должен быть короткий agent-facing doc, не handbook.

Структура:

- `What This Product Is`
- `Primary User`
- `Core Product Loop`
- `Major Product Areas`
- `Product Model Invariants`
- `When This Context Matters`

Содержательно туда перенести из research:

- app = seller workspace / mobile control plane
- seller operator persona
- core loop:
  - login
  - restore/choose supplier
  - enter seller workspace
  - review dashboard/tasks/notifications
  - act in catalog / logistics / buyers / analytics flows
  - re-enter via push/deeplink
- major product areas:
  - main dashboard
  - goods & prices
  - marketplace / deliveries
  - buyers
  - more
  - account lifecycle
- главный продуктовый инвариант:
  - supplier context — это ось продукта, а не второстепенный фильтр

В этом doc не нужно:
- codebase metrics
- lists of modules
- file:line evidence
- large risks tables

Допустимо в конце сделать короткую секцию:
- `Derived from local product-forensics research artifacts`
Но без ссылок на абсолютные пути вне текущего worktree.

### 3. Добавить `SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

Создать новый файл:
- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

Это должен быть operational reference для задач, затрагивающих shell/runtime behavior.

Структура:

- `When To Read This`
- `Root Shell Model`
- `Session And Auth Model`
- `Workspace And Tabs`
- `Feature Gating`
- `Deeplinks And Push Routing`
- `Supplier Context`
- `Practical Editing Warnings`

Содержательно туда поднять из L2:

- root shell centered on `RootCoordinator`
- launch path:
  - splash
  - preload config / permissions / account info
  - resolve auth / greeting / onboarding / no-suppliers / tabs
- auth/session truth is split across several state holders
- current workspace is a five-slot tab shell
- main dashboard is widget-based and mid-migration
- feature visibility often depends on both KVS and ABAC
- deeplink routing is split between new and legacy subsystems
- supplier switching can reset navigation and rebind permissions / push / network context
- selected supplier propagates into transport/runtime, not just UI

В конце добавить `Practical Editing Warnings` с короткими агентскими правилами:

- не предполагать статичную tab layout
- не предполагать единственный source of truth для auth state
- не менять feature visibility, не проверив KVS + ABAC + supplier context
- не считать deeplink handling централизованным в одном месте
- при изменениях supplier-related flows ожидать side effects в network / push / navigation

### 4. Обновить `Reference Map` и decision hooks в root `AGENTS.md`

В `Reference Map` добавить:

- product model and seller workspace context:
  `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`
- auth, shell, tabs, gating, deeplinks, supplier runtime context:
  `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

Также расширить один decision hook в root:

- если задача про auth, tabs, dashboard widgets, remote config, ABAC, deeplink, push routing, supplier switching или “почему пользователь видит/не видит экран”, сначала читать `SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`
- если задача про business intent feature/module, сначала читать `PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`

### 5. Явно зафиксировать границы между `.research` и agent docs

В рамках этого изменения выбрать такой контракт:

- `.research/*` остаётся raw research layer
- `AgenticDocs/*` — distilled, stable agent-facing layer
- root `AGENTS.md` — only execution-critical highlights

Это означает:

- не копировать в agent docs длинные tables, metrics, repo inventory и evidence chains
- не ссылаться из root `AGENTS.md` на `.research/*`
- не требовать наличия `.research` в task worktree для нормальной работы агента
- research использовать только как авторский source material при создании новых docs

Это важно, потому что текущие research files, которые ты показал, сейчас доступны из base checkout, но не являются надёжным repo-local reference слоем внутри текущего task worktree.

## Test Plan

Проверить качество новой структуры по 5 сценариям.

1. Root-only check
- прочитать только `AGENTS.md`
- убедиться, что агент понимает:
  - что это seller app
  - что supplier context важен
  - что shell dynamic and gated
  - куда идти за detail по shell/runtime

2. Product understanding check
- запросы вроде:
  - “что это за продукт?”
  - “что делает этот модуль для селлера?”
- должны естественно вести в `PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`, а не в `.research`

3. Runtime understanding check
- запросы вроде:
  - “почему пользователь не видит таб?”
  - “почему deeplink открыл не тот экран?”
  - “что может сломать supplier switch?”
- должны естественно вести в `SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

4. Context size check
- root `AGENTS.md` не должен заметно разрастись в handbook
- новые docs должны быть короткими operational syntheses, а не копией research artifacts

5. Dependency check
- в новых docs не должно быть зависимости на абсолютные пути из `/Users/iv/Developer/Wildberries/partners-app-dev/.research/...`
- агент должен понимать новые docs без доступа к base checkout

## Assumptions

- Новые product docs остаются обычными reference docs без `@`-include.
- Root `AGENTS.md` получает только короткий стабильный `Product Context`, а не подробную продуктовую документацию.
- Источником содержания служат L4/L3/L2 research artifacts, но финальные agent docs должны быть self-contained внутри текущего repo/worktree.
- L1 recon используется только как background source, не как прямой agent-facing doc.

The `request_user_input` tool is unavailable in Default mode. If you call it while in Default mode, it will return an error.

===== L7033 [user] =====
4. Create the worktree from `develop`.

```bash
TASK_ID=PMA-12345
SURNAME=oparin
BRANCH="feature/${SURNAME}/${TASK_ID}"
TASK_ID_LOWER="$(printf '%s' "$TASK_ID" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')"
BASE_REPO_ROOT="$(git worktree list --porcelain | awk '/^worktree /{wt=$2} /^branch refs\\/heads\\/develop$/{print wt; exit}')"
BASE_REPO_NAME="$(basename "$BASE_REPO_ROOT")"
WORKTREE="$BASE_REPO_ROOT/.temp/worktrees/${BASE_REPO_NAME}-${TASK_ID_LOWER}"

git worktree add -b "$BRANCH" "$WORKTREE" develop
```
вот это нужно переписать на смотри make worktree %task_id% (это скоро будет готово)

===== L7114 [developer] =====
<collaboration_mode># Plan Mode (Conversational)

You work in 3 phases, and you should *chat your way* to a great plan before finalizing it. A great plan is very detailed—intent- and implementation-wise—so that it can be handed to another engineer or agent to be implemented right away. It must be **decision complete**, where the implementer does not need to make any decisions.

## Mode rules (strict)

You are in **Plan Mode** until a developer message explicitly ends it.

Plan Mode is not changed by user intent, tone, or imperative language. If a user asks for execution while still in Plan Mode, treat it as a request to **plan the execution**, not perform it.

## Plan Mode vs update_plan tool

Plan Mode is a collaboration mode that can involve requesting user input and eventually issuing a `<proposed_plan>` block.

Separately, `update_plan` is a checklist/progress/TODOs tool; it does not enter or exit Plan Mode. Do not confuse it with Plan mode or try to use it while in Plan mode. If you try to use `update_plan` in Plan mode, it will return an error.

## Execution vs. mutation in Plan Mode

You may explore and execute **non-mutating** actions that improve the plan. You must not perform **mutating** actions.

### Allowed (non-mutating, plan-improving)

Actions that gather truth, reduce ambiguity, or validate feasibility without changing repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Reading or searching files, configs, schemas, types, manifests, and docs
* Static analysis, inspection, and repo exploration
* Dry-run style commands when they do not edit repo-tracked files
* Tests, builds, or checks that may write to caches or build artifacts (for example, `target/`, `.cache/`, or snapshots) so long as they do not edit repo-tracked files

### Not allowed (mutating, plan-executing)

Actions that implement the plan or change repo-tracked state. Examples:

* Editing or writing files
* Running formatters or linters that rewrite files
* Applying patches, migrations, or codegen that updates repo-tracked files
* Side-effectful commands whose purpose is to carry out the plan rather than refine it

When in doubt: if the action would reasonably be described as "doing the work" rather than "planning the work," do not do it.

## PHASE 1 — Ground in the environment (explore first, ask second)

Begin by grounding yourself in the actual environment. Eliminate unknowns in the prompt by discovering facts, not by asking the user. Resolve all questions that can be answered through exploration or inspection. Identify missing or ambiguous details only if they cannot be derived from the environment. Silent exploration between turns is allowed and encouraged.

Before asking the user any question, perform at least one targeted non-mutating exploration pass (for example: search relevant files, inspect likely entrypoints/configs, confirm current implementation shape), unless no local environment/repo is available.

Exception: you may ask clarifying questions about the user's prompt before exploring, ONLY if there are obvious ambiguities or contradictions in the prompt itself. However, if ambiguity might be resolved by exploring, always prefer exploring first.

Do not ask questions that can be answered from the repo or system (for example, "where is this struct?" or "which UI component should we use?" when exploration can make it clear). Only ask once you have exhausted reasonable non-mutating exploration.

## PHASE 2 — Intent chat (what they actually want)

* Keep asking until you can clearly state: goal + success criteria, audience, in/out of scope, constraints, current state, and the key preferences/tradeoffs.
* Bias toward questions over guessing: if any high-impact ambiguity remains, do NOT plan yet—ask.

## PHASE 3 — Implementation chat (what/how we’ll build)

* Once intent is stable, keep asking until the spec is decision complete: approach, interfaces (APIs/schemas/I/O), data flow, edge cases/failure modes, testing + acceptance criteria, rollout/monitoring, and any migrations/compat constraints.

## Asking questions

Critical rules:

* Strongly prefer using the `request_user_input` tool to ask any questions.
* Offer only meaningful multiple‑choice options; don’t include filler choices that are obviously wrong or irrelevant.
* In rare cases where an unavoidable, important question can’t be expressed with reasonable multiple‑choice options (due to extreme ambiguity), you may ask it directly without the tool.

You SHOULD ask many questions, but each question must:

* materially change the spec/plan, OR
* confirm/lock an assumption, OR
* choose between meaningful tradeoffs.
* not be answerable by non-mutating commands.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for decisions that materially change the plan, for confirming important assumptions, or for information that cannot be discovered via non-mutating exploration.

## Two kinds of unknowns (treat differently)

1. **Discoverable facts** (repo/system truth): explore first.

   * Before asking, run targeted searches and check likely sources of truth (configs/manifests/entrypoints/schemas/types/constants).
   * Ask only if: multiple plausible candidates; nothing found but you need a missing identifier/context; or ambiguity is actually product intent.
   * If asking, present concrete candidates (paths/service names) + recommend one.
   * Never ask questions you can answer from your environment (e.g., “where is this struct”).

2. **Preferences/tradeoffs** (not discoverable): ask early.

   * These are intent or implementation preferences that cannot be derived from exploration.
   * Provide 2–4 mutually exclusive options + a recommended default.
   * If unanswered, proceed with the recommended option and record it as an assumption in the final plan.

## Finalization rule

Only output the final plan when it is decision complete and leaves no decisions to the implementer.

When you present the official plan, wrap it in a `<proposed_plan>` block so the client can render it specially:

1) The opening tag must be on its own line.
2) Start the plan content on the next line (no text on the same line as the tag).
3) The closing tag must be on its own line.
4) Use Markdown inside the block.
5) Keep the tags exactly as `<proposed_plan>` and `</proposed_plan>` (do not translate or rename them), even if the plan content is in another language.

Example:

<proposed_plan>
plan content
</proposed_plan>

plan content should be human and agent digestible. The final plan must be plan-only, concise by default, and include:

* A clear title
* A brief summary section
* Important changes or additions to public APIs/interfaces/types
* Test cases and scenarios
* Explicit assumptions and defaults chosen where needed

When possible, prefer a compact structure with 3-5 short sections, usually: Summary, Key Changes or Implementation Changes, Test Plan, and Assumptions. Do not include a separate Scope section unless scope boundaries are genuinely important to avoid mistakes.

Prefer grouped implementation bullets by subsystem or behavior over file-by-file inventories. Mention files only when needed to disambiguate a non-obvious change, and avoid naming more than 3 paths unless extra specificity is necessary to prevent mistakes. Prefer behavior-level descriptions over symbol-by-symbol removal lists. For v1 feature-addition plans, do not invent detailed schema, validation, precedence, fallback, or wire-shape policy unless the request establishes it or it is needed to prevent a concrete implementation mistake; prefer the intended capability and minimum interface/behavior changes.

Keep bullets short and avoid explanatory sub-bullets unless they are needed to prevent ambiguity. Prefer the minimum detail needed for implementation safety, not exhaustive coverage. Within each section, compress related changes into a few high-signal bullets and omit branch-by-branch logic, repeated invariants, and long lists of unaffected behavior unless they are necessary to prevent a likely implementation mistake. Avoid repeated repo facts and irrelevant edge-case or rollout detail. For straightforward refactors, keep the plan to a compact summary, key edits, tests, and assumptions. If the user asks for more detail, then expand.

Do not ask "should I proceed?" in the final output. The user can easily switch out of Plan mode and request implementation if you have included a `<proposed_plan>` block in your response. Alternatively, they can decide to stay in Plan mode and continue refining the plan.

Only produce at most one `<proposed_plan>` block per turn, and only when you are presenting a complete spec.

If the user stays in Plan mode and asks for revisions after a prior `<proposed_plan>`, any new `<proposed_plan>` must be a complete replacement.
</collaboration_mode>

# Add a High-Level Project Map and Ownership Lookup Layer

## Summary

Add one more agent-facing reference layer that answers two recurring questions quickly:

- where in the repo a product area or runtime concern usually lives
- how to determine who owns a path or module without guessing

The implementation should introduce a new compact `PROJECT_MAP.md` in `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/`, derived from the existing product-forensics research for structure and from `CODEOWNERS` for ownership truth. Then wire it into root `AGENTS.md` so weak models can discover it from the root context.

This is a docs-only change. No changes to code, scripts, or repo workflows.

## Key Changes

### 1. Add `PROJECT_MAP.md` as the repo-surface map

Create a new file:

- `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/PROJECT_MAP.md`

Purpose:
- give the agent a short, stable mental map of the repository
- avoid sending the agent into raw `.research` or the full `CODEOWNERS` file for first-pass orientation

Recommended structure:

1. `When To Read This`
- use when the task starts with “where does this live?”, “which area owns this?”, “which stream is this in?”, “which top-level module group should I inspect first?”
- use before opening large parts of the repo blindly

2. `Top-Level Repository Map`
- short table or bullet map for:
  - `App/` — executable shell, app entry, app-level resources, DocC
  - `Core/` — reusable infra and platform services
  - `Utility/` — lightweight Apple-framework helpers/adapters
  - `Features/` — seller-facing product domains
  - `Widgets/` — iOS widgets
  - `Configurations/`, `Scripts/`, `Tuist/`, `Danger/`, `fastlane/` — support/build/release tooling
- keep each description to one line

3. `Feature Domain Map`
- list the actual top-level feature domains present in the repo:
  - `Advertisement`
  - `Chats`
  - `Content`
  - `Integrations`
  - `LK`
  - `Marketplace`
  - `Prices`
  - `RKI`
  - `Reviews`
  - `Supplies`
  - `Tabs`
  - `Features/Core`
- for each domain, add:
  - one short product meaning
  - one or two example paths when useful
- keep it high-level; do not enumerate all submodules

4. `Cross-Cutting Areas`
- call out the places weak models often misread:
  - `Features/Tabs/` is shell glue plus cross-stream surfaces
  - `Features/Core/` is feature-level shared/core UI, not the same as root `Core/`
  - `App/`, `Core/`, and some `Tabs` areas are part of shell/runtime/platform work, not business domains
- mention that some product streams own specific subpaths inside `Features/Tabs/...`

5. `Ownership Lookup`
- explicitly state that `CODEOWNERS` is the source of truth for ownership
- define the lookup rule:
  - use the most specific matching path in `CODEOWNERS`
  - do not infer final ownership from top-level folder name alone
- explain the practical model:
  - top-level domain gives a first-pass stream guess
  - `CODEOWNERS` confirms the actual responsible reviewers/owners
  - specific `Features/Tabs/...` or widget subpaths may belong to a product stream rather than Core
- include 3-5 concrete examples, for example:
  - `Features/Marketplace/...` -> Marketplace stream unless a deeper override exists
  - `Features/Tabs/Sources/AppSources/Tabs/Main/Widgets/News/` -> specific RKI-owned tab subpath
  - `App/`, `Core/`, `Utility/`, `Features/Core/` -> Core-owned surfaces unless `CODEOWNERS` says otherwise

6. `How To Use This With Stream Rules`
- clarify the connection to `STREAMS.md`
- stream/domain is a scope hint
- `CODEOWNERS` resolves ownership
- competency still controls whether the current developer may edit across streams

### 2. Update root `AGENTS.md` to surface this map

Add one short decision hook in `Active Task Execution`, for example:

- if the task starts with repo orientation, feature location, or ownership discovery, read `PROJECT_MAP.md` before exploring broadly

Add one line to `Reference Map`:

- repository surface, feature domains, and ownership lookup:
  `App/Documentation/AgenticDocs/PROJECT_MAP.md`

Do not add `@` includes.
Keep this as an on-demand reference doc, like the other `AgenticDocs` files.

### 3. Keep ownership truth in `CODEOWNERS`, not in research

Use the product-forensics files only for the high-level repo/domain explanation.

Use `CODEOWNERS` as the only ownership source of truth in the new doc.

That means:
- do not hardcode speculative “teams” from research
- do not duplicate large portions of `CODEOWNERS`
- do not try to maintain a second owner registry inside docs
- do not reference `.research` files from root `AGENTS.md`

The new doc may mention the coarse owner groups that already exist as section headers in `CODEOWNERS`:
- `Core`
- `Reviews`
- `LK`
- `Chats`
- `Content`
- `Integrations`
- `Prices`
- `RKI`
- `Advertisement`
- `Supplies`
- `Marketplace`

But it must frame them as:
- first-pass domain buckets
- overridden by more specific `CODEOWNERS` entries when applicable

### 4. Keep the doc compact and weak-model-safe

`PROJECT_MAP.md` should stay compact and operational.

Rules for the implementation:
- no file:line evidence
- no metrics
- no long module inventories
- no full ownership tables copied from `CODEOWNERS`
- no raw `.research` excerpts
- no attempt to document every feature module

It should read like:
- a short map
- a short lookup guide
- a short warning about cross-cutting shell areas

## Test Plan

Validate the new documentation against these scenarios:

1. Repo orientation
- A weak model reading `AGENTS.md` should discover `PROJECT_MAP.md` as the first stop for “where does this live?” questions.

2. Domain location
- For prompts like:
  - “where should I look for seller auth?”
  - “where does marketplace logic live?”
  - “where are dashboard widgets likely implemented?”
- `PROJECT_MAP.md` should narrow the search to the right top-level area without needing `.research`.

3. Ownership lookup
- For prompts like:
  - “who owns this path?”
  - “which stream is responsible for this module?”
- the doc should lead the agent to:
  - first-pass domain guess from the map
  - final confirmation via `CODEOWNERS`
- it should also prevent the common mistake of assuming all `Features/Tabs/...` code is Core-owned.

4. Cross-doc consistency
- `PROJECT_MAP.md` should not conflict with:
  - `ARCHITECTURE_RULES.md`
  - `STREAMS.md`
  - `PRODUCT_CONTEXT.md`
  - `SHELL_AND_RUNTIME_CONTEXT.md`

5. Context discipline
- The new doc should be readable in one pass and stay significantly smaller than raw research or `CODEOWNERS`.

## Assumptions

- `PROJECT_MAP.md` is the right granularity; a full “team ownership registry” is intentionally out of scope.
- Ownership remains repo-tracked in `CODEOWNERS`; the new doc is a guide, not a second source of truth.
- The high-level map will use the actual current top-level feature domains present in `Features/`.
- `Features/Tabs` and `Features/Core` must be treated as special cross-cutting areas, not simple business-domain folders.
- `.research` remains source material only and is not referenced directly from root `AGENTS.md`.
</proposed_plan>

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

merge-request-check:
  extends:
    - .mr_or_local
  stage: validate
  variables:
    DANGER_SWIFT_NO_UPDATE_CHECK: "true"
    DANGER_ID: "merge-request-check"
  before_script:
    - !reference [.token_substitution, before_script]
    - !reference [.select_xcode, before_script]
  script:
    - export DANGER_CHECK_MERGE_REQUEST=true
    - echo -e "section_start:`date +%s`:danger[collapsed=true]\r\x1B[0K\x1B[0K\x1B[36;1mDanger job\x1B[0;m\x1B[0;m"
    - args=""
    - >
      if [ "${GITLAB_CI}" = "true" ]; then
          PATH="${HOME}/.local/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:${PATH}" danger-swift ci --id=merge-request-check --failOnErrors $args
      else
          PATH="${HOME}/.local/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:${PATH}" danger-swift local --id=merge-request-check --base "${TARGET_BRANCH:-main}" --failOnErrors
      fi
    - echo -e "section_end:`date +%s`:danger\r\x1B[0K"
  dependencies: []

    - if: $CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE == 'merge_request_event' && ($CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TITLE =~ /DANGER_SKIP/ || $DANGER_SKIP == "true" || $CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TARGET_BRANCH_NAME != $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH)
      allow_failure: true
    - !reference [.mr, rules]
  cache:

      paths:

  when: always

core-approve:
  extends:
    - .mr
  stage: validate
  needs: []
  before_script:
    - !reference [.prepare_brew, before_script]
  script:
    - CORE_TEAM=$(grep -m1 '^\[Core\]' CODEOWNERS | cut -d' ' -f2- | tr '@' ' ' | sort)
    - 'APPROVERS=$(curl -s --header "PRIVATE-TOKEN: $GITLAB_API_PRIVATE_TOKEN" "$CI_API_V4_URL/projects/$CI_PROJECT_ID/merge_requests/$CI_MERGE_REQUEST_IID/approvals" | jq -r ".approved_by[].user.username" | sort)'
    - CORE_APPROVERS=$(comm -12 <(echo "$CORE_TEAM" | tr ' ' '\n' | sort) <(echo "$APPROVERS" | tr ' ' '\n' | sort))
    - 'if [ -n "$CORE_APPROVERS" ]; then exit 0; else echo "Необходимо получить approve от любого из core: $CORE_TEAM"; exit 1; fi'
  rules:
    - if: $CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TITLE =~ /DANGER_SKIP/ && $CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TITLE !~ /MIRRORS_UPDATE/

===== L7461 [assistant] =====
Да, в целом стиль уже хороший и в основном соответствует нашему weak-model-safe паттерну. Проверил и сам `SKILL.md`, и реальный surface `gmr` wrapper: команды из skill совпадают с тем, что умеет [`gmr`](/Users/iv/agents/skills/glab-mr-workflow/scripts/gmr).

Главный finding один:

- High: в fast path skill велит вызывать auth-check как `<ensure-command> <mr-target>` ([SKILL.md:71](/Users/iv/agents/skills/glab-mr-workflow/SKILL.md#L71)), но сам helper принимает только `hostname-or-url` ([ensure-glab-auth.sh:6](/Users/iv/agents/skills/glab-mr-workflow/scripts/ensure-glab-auth.sh#L6)). Для full MR URL это ещё работает, а для IID-only запроса это ложный путь: `123` будет интерпретирован как hostname ([ensure-glab-auth.sh:17](/Users/iv/agents/skills/glab-mr-workflow/scripts/ensure-glab-auth.sh#L17)). При этом сам `gmr` умеет корректно дорезолвить IID в `host/repo` через remote context ([gmr_main.py:145](/Users/iv/agents/skills/glab-mr-workflow/scripts/gmr_main.py#L145)). То есть weak model может получить фальшивую “auth missing” ошибку там, где проблемы нет.

Остальное скорее сильные стороны:
- есть `Default Mode`, `Resolve Context First`, `Ask Only If Blocked`, `Output Contract`, `Pre-final Check`
- есть явный `execute, don’t instruct`
- есть абсолютные command paths
- есть узкий happy path для `MR status` и pipeline diagnostics
- есть разделение между `gmr` для agent-facing reads и `glab` для стандартных writes

То есть мой вердикт: **почти да, подходит**, но этот один auth/context bug я бы обязательно исправил перед тем, как считать skill fully weak-model-safe. После него я бы ещё слегка дожал только одну вещь: в review section явно сказать “reuse the already resolved MR context for raw `glab` calls”, чтобы слабая модель не начинала заново гадать `iid/repo/host`.
